Observe → Discover → Understand → Decide → Act → Monitor → Learn → Improve

Assessment

This document establishes several architectural governance decisions that should now be treated as frozen.

1. Phase numbering is immutable

This is the correct decision.

Once implementation files, validation reports, repository history, and documentation reference phase numbers, renumbering them would break:

implementation traceability
audit history
source code metadata
documentation lineage
repository consistency

Institutionally, freezing completed phase numbers is the correct governance model.

2. Separation into Kernel and Enablement blocks

This is a much cleaner execution model.

Instead of mixing infrastructure with business functionality, Stage-25 now becomes:

Stage-25

Block-A
COREI Platform Kernel

↓

Block-B
COREI Platform Enablement

This matches how mature operating systems evolve.

First build reusable runtime infrastructure.

Then build user-facing functionality on top.

3. Kernel-first architecture

The reasoning is technically sound.

For example:

Trading Experience

↓

Workspace

↓

Capabilities

↓

Providers

↓

Foundation

cannot exist unless every lower layer already exists.

The same applies to:

runtime
lifecycle
state
events
dependency resolution
orchestration

These are kernel responsibilities rather than business responsibilities.

4. Missing kernel platforms

The addition of the remaining kernel platforms is architecturally justified.

Module Platform

Owns modular functional units.

Engine Platform

Owns execution engines.

Domain Platform

Owns institutional business domains.

Platform Runtime Integration

Owns cross-platform runtime coordination.

Event Platform

Owns institutional event governance.

State Platform

Owns global state governance beyond local platform state.

Extension Platform

Owns extensibility and plugin architecture.

These are all cross-cutting concerns that should exist as first-class platforms rather than being scattered across the repository.

5. Original roadmap preservation

A particularly strong aspect is that the original roadmap is not discarded.

Instead:

it is deferred,
renumbering is avoided,
repository continuity is preserved.

That prevents architectural churn.

The resulting dependency chain becomes:

One recommendation

I recommend making one governance rule explicit and treating it as a permanent architectural law:

No platform in Block-B may introduce foundational runtime infrastructure that properly belongs in Block-A.

In practical terms:

If, during Phase-21, you discover a generic runtime service that every platform would need, do not implement it inside Phase-21.
Instead, classify it as a missing kernel capability and evaluate whether it belongs in one of the existing kernel platforms (or, only if absolutely necessary, a newly approved kernel platform before kernel freeze).

This prevents higher-level business phases from accumulating hidden infrastructure and preserves the clean separation between the operating-system kernel and platform enablement.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-10 → STEP-01
Module Platform Foundation

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-01 establishes the Module Platform Foundation, the institutional governance layer responsible for defining, registering, and managing functional Modules within the COREI Operating System.

While the Service Platform (Phase-09) introduced executable institutional Services, the Module Platform introduces the bounded organizational layer that groups related Services into reusable functional Modules.

The Module Platform becomes the architectural boundary between the Service Platform and the future Domain Platform while remaining independent of business logic, runtime execution, orchestration, and domain implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Foundation Platform
        │
        ▼
Provider Platform
        │
        ▼
Capability Platform
        │
        ▼
Workspace Platform
        │
        ▼
Experience Platform
        │
        ▼
Application Platform
        │
        ▼
Service Platform
        │
        ▼
Module Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Module Platform consumes institutional Services and provides reusable functional Modules for the future Domain Platform.

3. Purpose

The Module Platform answers the institutional question:

"How are institutional Services organized into reusable functional Modules?"

Rather than allowing Applications or Domains to manage individual Services directly, COREI introduces Modules as bounded organizational units.

Examples include:

Trading Module
Market Module
Portfolio Module
Risk Module
Infrastructure Module
Identity Module
Audit Module
Notification Module

A Module owns Services.

It does not own:

Domains
Business workflows
Runtime execution
Provider implementations
User interfaces

Those responsibilities remain within their respective institutional platforms.

4. Module Platform Components

The Module Platform Foundation introduces five institutional governance components.

Module Contract

Defines the institutional contract that every Module must satisfy.

Responsibilities include:

Module identity
Module name
Version
Enablement state

The Module Contract establishes the canonical interface for every Module implemented within COREI.

Module Model

Represents the runtime definition of a Module.

The Module Model extends the Module Contract while remaining completely independent of implementation.

The Module Model becomes the canonical runtime representation stored inside the Module Registry.

Module Registry

Provides the authoritative repository of all registered Modules.

Responsibilities include:

Module registration
Module discovery
Module lookup
Module enumeration

The Registry becomes the single source of truth for Module definitions.

Module Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Module Registry.

Responsibilities include:

Register Module
Retrieve Modules
Enumerate Modules
Registry access

Operational logic remains isolated from Registry storage.

Module Runtime

Represents the runtime state of the Module Platform itself.

Current responsibility:

Platform initialization state

Future steps extend runtime governance without modifying the architectural responsibility of the Runtime Platform.

5. Module Platform Model

The institutional Module Platform is represented as:

Module Platform
        │
        ├── Module Contract
        ├── Module Model
        ├── Module Registry
        ├── Module Manager
        └── Module Runtime

These components collectively establish the foundational governance layer for every Module within COREI.

6. Relationship with the Service Platform

The Module Platform consumes Services as reusable organizational building blocks.

Relationship:

Module
        │

------------------

STAGE-25 → PHASE-10 → STEP-02
Module Lifecycle Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-02 establishes the Module Lifecycle Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the deterministic operational lifecycle of every Module within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-01 introduced the identity and governance of Modules, STEP-02 introduces the standardized lifecycle model that governs how Modules transition through operational states during their existence.

The Module Lifecycle Platform defines lifecycle semantics only. It remains independent of runtime execution, dependency resolution, orchestration, monitoring, state management, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Module Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Lifecycle Platform becomes the second institutional layer of the Module Platform.

3. Purpose

The Module Lifecycle Platform answers the institutional question:

"What is the current operational lifecycle stage of this Module?"

It establishes a deterministic lifecycle contract shared by every Module throughout the COREI Operating System.

The Module Lifecycle Platform introduces:

Module Lifecycle Stages
Module Lifecycle Contract
Default Module Lifecycle
Module Lifecycle Registry
Module Lifecycle Manager
Module Lifecycle Public API

It is not responsible for:

runtime execution
dependency resolution
service initialization
orchestration
monitoring
business logic

These responsibilities remain delegated to later Module Platform layers.

4. Module Lifecycle Components

The Module Lifecycle Platform introduces five institutional components.

Module Lifecycle Stage

Represents the standardized lifecycle states available to every Module.

The lifecycle stages include:

Registered
Initializing
Initialized
Starting
Running
Suspending
Suspended
Resuming
Stopping
Stopped
Unloading
Unloaded
Failed

These stages define the canonical lifecycle vocabulary for all Module implementations.

Module Lifecycle Contract

Defines the institutional lifecycle interface implemented by every Module.

The contract encapsulates the current lifecycle stage without prescribing execution behavior.

It provides a common lifecycle representation across the platform.

Default Module Lifecycle

Provides the initial lifecycle definition assigned to newly registered Modules.

The default lifecycle establishes a deterministic starting point for lifecycle progression.

This ensures every Module begins from an identical operational state.

Module Lifecycle Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for all Module lifecycle definitions.

Responsibilities include:

lifecycle registration
lifecycle discovery
lifecycle enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative source for Module lifecycle definitions.

Module Lifecycle Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Lifecycle Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register lifecycle
retrieve lifecycle definitions
enumerate registered lifecycles

Operational management remains isolated from registry storage.

5. Lifecycle Model

The institutional Module Lifecycle is represented as:

Module Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Lifecycle Stage

Every Module exposes exactly one lifecycle definition.

The lifecycle definition describes operational state only.

Execution remains outside the scope of the Lifecycle Platform.

6. Relationship with the Module Foundation

The Lifecycle Platform extends the Module Foundation introduced in STEP-01.

Relationship:

Module Foundation
        │
        ▼
Module Lifecycle

The Foundation establishes Module identity.

The Lifecycle establishes Module operational progression.

7. Relationship with the Runtime Platform

The Lifecycle Platform defines what operational stage a Module is in.

The Runtime Platform defines how a Module executes.

Relationship:

Module Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Module Runtime

Lifecycle governs operational state.

Runtime governs execution.

The two remain architecturally independent.

8. Relationship with the Future Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Orchestrator will consume the Lifecycle Platform when coordinating Module execution.

Relationship:

Module Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Runtime Orchestrator

The Lifecycle Platform supplies lifecycle information.

The Runtime Orchestrator performs deterministic operational coordination.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-10 → STEP-03
Module State Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-03 establishes the Module State Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the canonical operational state model for every Module within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-02 introduced the deterministic operational lifecycle of a Module, STEP-03 introduces the operational state model that represents the current condition of a Module during its execution.

The Module State Platform provides standardized contracts for operational readiness, health, availability, and synchronization while remaining completely independent of lifecycle progression, runtime execution, dependency management, orchestration, monitoring, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Module Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The State Platform becomes the third institutional layer of the Module Platform.

3. Purpose

The Module State Platform answers the institutional question:

"What is the current operational condition of this Module?"

It establishes a deterministic state model shared by every Module throughout the COREI Operating System.

The Module State Platform introduces:

Module Operational Status
Module Health Status
Module State Contract
Default Module State
Module State Registry
Module State Manager
Module State Public API

It is not responsible for:

lifecycle progression
runtime execution
dependency resolution
orchestration
monitoring implementation
business logic

These responsibilities remain delegated to their respective institutional platforms.

4. Module State Components

The Module State Platform introduces six institutional components.

Module Operational Status

Represents the operational readiness of a Module.

The operational status defines whether the Module is:

Unknown
Ready
Not Ready
Degraded
Failed

This status represents operational availability rather than execution.

Module Health Status

Represents the health condition of a Module.

The health status defines whether the Module is:

Unknown
Healthy
Unhealthy
Degraded

Health remains independent of lifecycle progression.

Module State Contract

Defines the canonical operational state interface implemented by every Module.

The contract standardizes:

Operational Status
Health Status
Availability
Synchronization

The Module State becomes the authoritative operational state representation for every Module.

Default Module State

Provides the initial operational state assigned to newly created Modules.

The default state establishes a deterministic baseline before any runtime activity occurs.

Every Module begins from the same institutional operational state.

Module State Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Module state definitions.

Responsibilities include:

state registration
state discovery
state enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Module state definitions.

Module State Manager

Provides operational interaction with the State Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register state
retrieve states
enumerate registered states

Operational management remains separated from registry storage.

5. Module State Model

The institutional Module State is represented as:

Module State
        │
        ├── Operational Status
        ├── Health Status
        ├── Availability
        └── Synchronization

Every Module exposes exactly one operational state model.

The State Platform describes operational condition only.

Execution remains outside its scope.

6. Relationship with the Module Lifecycle Platform

The Lifecycle Platform defines the operational progression of a Module.

The State Platform defines the current operational condition of a Module.

Relationship:

Module Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Module State

Lifecycle answers:

Where is the Module in its lifecycle?

State answers:

What is the current operational condition of the Module?

These responsibilities remain independent.

7. Relationship with the Runtime Platform

The Runtime Platform consumes the Module State during execution.

Relationship:

Module State
        │
        ▼
Module Runtime

The State Platform supplies operational state.

The Runtime Platform performs execution.

The Runtime Platform does not own state definitions.

8. Relationship with the Future Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Orchestrator consumes Module State when coordinating execution decisions.

Relationship:

Module State
        │
        ▼
Runtime Orchestrator

The State Platform supplies operational state.

The Runtime Orchestrator coordinates deterministic execution using that information.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-10 → STEP-04
Module Composition Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-04 establishes the Module Composition Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the structural composition of every Module within the COREI Operating System.

While the previous steps established Module identity, lifecycle, and operational state, STEP-04 introduces the standardized composition model that defines which Services belong to a Module.

The Module Composition Platform provides a declarative structural representation of Module contents while remaining completely independent of dependency management, runtime execution, orchestration, lifecycle progression, operational state, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Module Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Composition Platform becomes the fourth institutional layer of the Module Platform.

3. Purpose

The Module Composition Platform answers the institutional question:

"Which Services structurally belong to this Module?"

Rather than allowing arbitrary Service grouping, COREI introduces a deterministic composition model that standardizes Module structure across the platform.

The Module Composition Platform introduces:

Module Service Reference
Module Composition Contract
Default Module Composition
Module Composition Registry
Module Composition Manager
Module Composition Public API

It is not responsible for:

dependency relationships
execution sequencing
lifecycle transitions
operational state
runtime behavior
orchestration
business logic

These responsibilities remain delegated to later Module Platform layers.

4. Module Composition Components

The Module Composition Platform introduces five institutional components.

Module Service Reference

Represents a structural reference to a Service owned by a Module.

Each reference defines:

Service Identifier
Service Name

The reference establishes ownership only.

It does not describe execution order or dependencies.

Module Composition Contract

Defines the canonical structural composition interface implemented by every Module.

The composition contract standardizes:

Service collection

Every Module exposes a single authoritative structural composition.

Default Module Composition

Provides the default structural composition assigned to newly created Modules.

Initially the Module contains no Services.

Services are introduced through future composition updates without modifying the foundational contract.

Module Composition Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Module compositions.

Responsibilities include:

composition registration
composition discovery
composition enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of structural Module definitions.

Module Composition Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Composition Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register composition
retrieve compositions
enumerate registered compositions

Management remains isolated from repository storage.

5. Module Composition Model

The institutional Module Composition is represented as:

Module Composition
        │
        ▼
Service References
        │
        ├── Service
        ├── Service
        ├── Service
        └── Service

The Composition Platform defines structural ownership only.

It does not define execution relationships.

6. Relationship with the Service Platform

The Composition Platform consumes Services from the Service Platform.

Relationship:

Module
        │
        ▼
Service References
        │
        ▼
Service Platform

The Service Platform owns Service implementation.

The Module Platform owns Service organization.

7. Relationship with the Future Dependency Platform

The Composition Platform establishes structural ownership.

The Dependency Platform establishes dependency relationships.

Relationship:

Module Composition
        │
        ▼
Module Dependency

Composition answers:

Which Services belong to this Module?

Dependency answers:

How do those Services depend upon one another?

The two responsibilities remain architecturally independent.

8. Relationship with the Runtime Platform

The Runtime Platform consumes the structural composition when creating Module runtime instances.

Relationship:

Module Composition
        │
        ▼
Module Runtime

Composition provides structure.

Runtime provides execution.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-10 → STEP-05
Module Dependency Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-05 establishes the Module Dependency Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the canonical dependency model for every Module within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-04 introduced the structural composition of a Module, STEP-05 introduces the deterministic dependency model that defines which institutional Modules a Module depends upon.

The Module Dependency Platform provides standardized dependency contracts while remaining completely independent of structural composition, lifecycle progression, operational state, runtime execution, orchestration, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Module Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Dependency Platform becomes the fifth institutional layer of the Module Platform.

3. Purpose

The Module Dependency Platform answers the institutional question:

"Which institutional Modules must exist before this Module can operate?"

Rather than allowing implicit or scattered dependency declarations, COREI introduces a deterministic dependency model shared by every Module.

The Module Dependency Platform introduces:

Module Dependency Reference
Module Dependency Contract
Default Module Dependency
Module Dependency Registry
Module Dependency Manager
Module Dependency Public API

It is not responsible for:

structural ownership
runtime execution
dependency resolution
orchestration
lifecycle management
operational state
business logic

These responsibilities remain delegated to later Module Platform layers.

4. Module Dependency Components

The Module Dependency Platform introduces five institutional components.

Module Dependency Reference

Represents a declarative reference to another institutional Module.

Each reference defines:

Module Identifier
Module Name

The reference establishes dependency identity only.

It does not establish execution order.

Module Dependency Contract

Defines the canonical dependency interface implemented by every Module.

The dependency contract standardizes:

Module dependency collection

Every Module exposes one authoritative dependency declaration.

Default Module Dependency

Provides the default dependency definition assigned to newly created Modules.

Initially every Module has no dependencies.

Dependencies are introduced during platform composition without modifying the foundational contract.

Module Dependency Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Module dependency definitions.

Responsibilities include:

dependency registration
dependency discovery
dependency enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Module dependency definitions.

Module Dependency Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Dependency Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register dependency
retrieve dependency definitions
enumerate registered dependencies

Operational management remains isolated from registry storage.

5. Module Dependency Model

The institutional Module Dependency is represented as:

Module Dependency
        │
        ▼
Dependency References
        │
        ├── Module
        ├── Module
        ├── Module
        └── Module

The Dependency Platform defines dependency declarations only.

It does not define dependency resolution or execution.

6. Relationship with the Module Composition Platform

The Composition Platform defines structural ownership.

The Dependency Platform defines operational dependency.

Relationship:

Module Composition
        │
        ▼
Module Dependency

Composition answers:

Which Services belong to this Module?

Dependency answers:

Which Modules must exist before this Module can operate?

These responsibilities remain completely independent.

7. Relationship with the Runtime Platform

The Runtime Platform consumes Module Dependency information during initialization.

Relationship:

Module Dependency
        │
        ▼
Module Runtime

The Dependency Platform supplies dependency declarations.

The Runtime Platform performs dependency resolution.

Dependency declarations remain independent of execution.

8. Relationship with the Runtime Graph Platform

The Runtime Graph Platform consumes Module Dependency definitions to construct deterministic execution topology.

Relationship:

Module Dependency
        │
        ▼
Module Runtime Graph

Dependency defines:

declared relationships

Runtime Graph defines:

complete dependency topology

The Dependency Platform remains declarative.

Module Runtime Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-06 establishes the Module Runtime Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the deterministic runtime environment for every Module within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-05 introduced declarative dependency definitions, STEP-06 introduces the standardized runtime contracts that represent the execution environment of a Module.

The Module Runtime Platform provides a unified runtime model that encapsulates runtime identity, execution context, runtime registration, and runtime management while remaining independent of dependency topology, runtime graph construction, orchestration, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Module Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Platform becomes the sixth institutional layer of the Module Platform.

3. Purpose

The Module Runtime Platform answers the institutional question:

"How is this Module represented during execution?"

Rather than allowing each Module to define its own execution representation, COREI introduces a standardized runtime model shared across the platform.

The Module Runtime Platform introduces:

Module Runtime Context
Module Runtime Contract
Default Module Runtime
Module Runtime Registry
Module Runtime Manager
Module Runtime Public API

It is not responsible for:

dependency graph construction
dependency resolution
execution scheduling
orchestration
business logic
lifecycle management
operational state

These responsibilities remain delegated to their respective institutional platforms.

4. Module Runtime Components

The Module Runtime Platform introduces five institutional components.

Module Runtime Context

Represents the execution context of a Module.

The Runtime Context defines:

Runtime Identifier
Runtime Start Time

The Runtime Context provides a deterministic execution identity for every Module instance.

Module Runtime Contract

Defines the canonical runtime interface implemented by every Module.

The Runtime Contract standardizes:

Initialization Status
Runtime Context

Every Module exposes a single runtime representation.

Default Module Runtime

Provides the default runtime assigned to newly created Modules.

Initially every Module runtime is:

not initialized
assigned an empty runtime identifier
assigned a deterministic default timestamp

Runtime values evolve during execution without modifying the runtime contract.

Module Runtime Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Module runtime definitions.

Responsibilities include:

runtime registration
runtime discovery
runtime enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Module runtime instances.

Module Runtime Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Runtime Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register runtime
retrieve runtimes
enumerate registered runtimes

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

5. Module Runtime Model

The institutional Module Runtime is represented as:

Module Runtime
        │
        ├── Initialized
        │
        └── Runtime Context
                 │
                 ├── Runtime Identifier
                 └── Started Time

The Runtime Platform defines execution representation only.

It does not define execution sequencing.

6. Relationship with the Module Dependency Platform

The Dependency Platform declares what a Module depends upon.

The Runtime Platform provides the execution environment after dependencies have been resolved.

Relationship:

Module Dependency
        │
        ▼
Module Runtime

Dependency answers:

What does the Module require?

Runtime answers:

How is the Module represented during execution?

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

7. Relationship with the Future Runtime Graph Platform

The Runtime Graph Platform consumes runtime definitions when constructing deterministic runtime topology.

Relationship:

Module Runtime
        │
        ▼
Module Runtime Graph

The Runtime Platform supplies execution entities.

The Runtime Graph connects those entities into a deterministic topology.

8. Relationship with the Future Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Orchestrator consumes Module Runtime definitions when coordinating execution.

Relationship:

Module Runtime
        │
        ▼
Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Platform provides execution identity.

The Runtime Orchestrator coordinates deterministic execution.

The Runtime Platform performs no orchestration.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-10 → STEP-07
Module Integration Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-07 establishes the Module Integration Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining how every Module integrates with lower institutional platforms within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-06 introduced the runtime representation of a Module, STEP-07 introduces the standardized integration model that declares the institutional platforms consumed by a Module.

The Module Integration Platform provides deterministic integration contracts while remaining independent of dependency topology, runtime graph construction, runtime orchestration, execution scheduling, monitoring, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Module Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Integration Platform becomes the seventh institutional layer of the Module Platform.

3. Purpose

The Module Integration Platform answers the institutional question:

"Which institutional platform components does this Module consume?"

Rather than embedding infrastructure references throughout the Module implementation, COREI centralizes platform integration into a deterministic integration model.

The Module Integration Platform introduces:

Module Provider Integration
Module Capability Integration
Module Service Integration
Module Integration Contract
Default Module Integration
Module Integration Registry
Module Integration Manager
Module Integration Public API

It is not responsible for:

dependency topology
runtime execution
runtime scheduling
runtime orchestration
lifecycle management
operational state
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to their respective institutional platforms.

4. Module Integration Components

The Module Integration Platform introduces seven institutional components.

Module Provider Integration

Represents the Provider Platform dependencies consumed by a Module.

Examples include:

Storage Providers
Messaging Providers
Authentication Providers
External Infrastructure Providers

Provider Integration remains declarative.

It does not initialize Providers.

Module Capability Integration

Represents reusable institutional Capabilities consumed by a Module.

Examples include:

Validation Capability
Configuration Capability
Security Capability
Logging Capability

Capability Integration defines consumption only.

Execution remains external.

Module Service Integration

Represents Services consumed by the Module.

Unlike Module Composition, which defines owned Services, Service Integration defines consumed Services originating outside the Module boundary.

Module Integration Contract

Defines the canonical integration interface implemented by every Module.

The integration contract standardizes:

Provider Integration
Capability Integration
Service Integration

Every Module exposes a single integration definition.

Default Module Integration

Provides the default integration assigned to newly created Modules.

Initially every integration collection is empty.

Platform integrations are introduced incrementally without modifying the foundational contract.

Module Integration Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Module integration definitions.

Responsibilities include:

integration registration
integration discovery
integration enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Module integration definitions.

Module Integration Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Integration Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register integration
retrieve integration definitions
enumerate registered integrations

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

5. Module Integration Model

The institutional Module Integration is represented as:

Module Integration
        │
        ├── Provider Integration
        │
        ├── Capability Integration
        │
        └── Service Integration

The Integration Platform defines platform relationships only.

It does not perform initialization.

6. Relationship with the Provider Platform

Modules consume Provider abstractions rather than infrastructure implementations.

Relationship:

Provider Platform
        │
        ▼
Module Integration

The Provider Platform owns infrastructure abstraction.

The Module Platform owns Provider consumption.

7. Relationship with the Capability Platform

The Module Platform consumes reusable Capabilities through the Integration Platform.

Relationship:

Capability Platform
        │
        ▼
Module Integration

Capabilities remain reusable institutional building blocks.

The Module Platform declares their usage.

8. Relationship with the Service Platform

The Service Platform exposes executable Services.

The Module Platform consumes those Services through Integration.

Relationship:

Service Platform
        │
        ▼
Module Integration

The Service Platform owns Service implementation.

The Module Platform owns Service consumption.

9. Relationship with the Runtime Graph Platform

The Runtime Graph Platform consumes integration declarations when constructing Module execution topology.

Relationship:

Module Integration
        │
        ▼
Module Runtime Graph

Integration defines external platform relationships.

The Runtime Graph converts those declarations into deterministic topology.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-10 → STEP-08
Module Runtime Graph Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-08 establishes the Module Runtime Graph Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the deterministic dependency topology of every Module within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-07 introduced declarative platform integration, STEP-08 consolidates all Module relationships into a unified Runtime Graph.

The Module Runtime Graph Platform introduces standardized graph contracts while remaining completely independent of runtime execution, orchestration, lifecycle management, operational state, monitoring, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Module Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Graph Platform becomes the eighth institutional layer of the Module Platform.

3. Purpose

The Module Runtime Graph Platform answers the institutional question:

"What is the complete dependency topology of this Module?"

While previous Module Platform layers declare identity, structure, dependencies, runtime, and integrations independently, the Runtime Graph Platform unifies those declarations into one authoritative dependency topology.

The Module Runtime Graph Platform introduces:

Module Runtime Node
Module Runtime Edge
Module Runtime Graph
Module Runtime Graph Registry
Module Runtime Graph Public API

It is not responsible for:

runtime execution
dependency initialization
lifecycle transitions
orchestration
scheduling
monitoring
business logic

These responsibilities remain delegated to the Runtime Platform and the Runtime Orchestrator.

4. Module Runtime Graph Components

The Runtime Graph Platform introduces four institutional components.

Module Runtime Node

Represents a node participating in a Module dependency graph.

Each Runtime Node defines:

Node Identifier
Node Name

Examples include:

Module
Provider
Capability
Service

Nodes represent graph entities only.

No execution behavior is associated with a Runtime Node.

Module Runtime Edge

Represents a dependency relationship between two Runtime Nodes.

Each Runtime Edge defines:

Source Node
Target Node

Edges establish deterministic dependency relationships without executing them.

Module Runtime Graph

Represents the complete dependency topology of a Module.

Each Runtime Graph contains:

Runtime Nodes
Runtime Edges

The Runtime Graph becomes the authoritative dependency topology for that Module.

Module Runtime Graph Registry

Represents the institutional registry of all Module Runtime Graphs within the COREI Operating System.

Responsibilities include:

graph registration
graph discovery
graph enumeration

The Registry provides centralized access to graph definitions while remaining completely independent of execution.

5. Module Runtime Graph Model

The institutional Runtime Graph is represented as:

Module Runtime Graph
        │
        ├── Nodes
        │
        └── Edges

The graph consolidates all dependency declarations into a deterministic topology.

6. Relationship with the Integration Platform

The Runtime Graph consumes declarations from all Module integration layers.

Relationship:

Provider Integration
          │
Capability Integration
          │
Service Integration
          │
Dependency Platform
          │
          ▼
Module Runtime Graph

The Runtime Graph answers:

"How are all declared Module relationships connected?"

The Integration Platform declares relationships.

The Runtime Graph consolidates those declarations into a complete dependency topology.

7. Relationship with the Runtime Platform

The Runtime Graph defines topology.

The Runtime Platform defines execution representation.

Relationship:

Module Runtime Graph
        │
        ▼
Module Runtime

The Runtime Graph answers:

What should execute?

The Runtime Platform answers:

How is it represented during execution?

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

8. Relationship with the Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Orchestrator consumes the Runtime Graph to determine deterministic execution order.

Relationship:

Module Runtime Graph
        │
        ▼
Module Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Graph provides dependency topology.

The Runtime Orchestrator performs execution coordination.

1. Objective

STEP-09 establishes the Module Runtime Orchestrator Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for coordinating the deterministic execution of every Module within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-08 introduced the Module Runtime Graph that models dependency topology, STEP-09 introduces the Runtime Orchestrator that consumes that topology and coordinates Module execution in a deterministic, repeatable, and auditable manner.

The Module Runtime Orchestrator Platform provides standardized orchestration contracts while remaining independent of dependency modeling, runtime representation, lifecycle definitions, monitoring, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Module Platform
        │

        ├── Lifecycle

        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Orchestrator becomes the ninth and final institutional layer of the Module Platform.

3. Purpose

The Module Runtime Orchestrator answers the institutional question:

"How should Modules execute in a deterministic order?"

Unlike the Runtime Graph, which models relationships, the Runtime Orchestrator coordinates execution using that topology.

The Module Runtime Orchestrator introduces:

Module Runtime Orchestrator Context
Module Runtime Orchestrator Contract
Default Module Runtime Orchestrator
Module Runtime Orchestrator Registry
Module Runtime Orchestrator Manager
Module Runtime Orchestrator Public API

It is not responsible for:

dependency modeling
dependency topology
lifecycle definitions
runtime identity
monitoring
business logic

Those responsibilities remain within their respective institutional platforms.

4. Module Runtime Orchestrator Components

The Runtime Orchestrator Platform introduces five institutional components.

Module Runtime Orchestrator Context

Represents the execution context used by the Runtime Orchestrator.

The context standardizes:

Execution Identifier
Execution Start Time

Every orchestration session is represented by a deterministic execution context.

Module Runtime Orchestrator Contract

Defines the canonical orchestration interface implemented by the Module Platform.

The contract standardizes:

Runtime Orchestrator Context

Execution algorithms remain external to the contract.

Default Module Runtime Orchestrator

Provides the default orchestration definition assigned to newly created Module runtimes.

Initially every Runtime Orchestrator contains:

empty execution identifier
deterministic default execution timestamp

Execution state evolves during runtime without modifying the foundational contract.

Module Runtime Orchestrator Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Runtime Orchestrator definitions.

Responsibilities include:

orchestrator registration
orchestrator discovery
orchestrator enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Module Runtime Orchestrators.

Module Runtime Orchestrator Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Runtime Orchestrator Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register orchestrator
retrieve orchestrators
enumerate registered orchestrators

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

        ├── Stop
        └── Unload

The Runtime Orchestrator coordinates execution only.

It does not define dependency topology.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-11 → STEP-01
Engine Foundation Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-01 establishes the Engine Foundation Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the identity, ownership, registration, and governance model for every Engine within the COREI Operating System.

While the Module Platform provides the structural execution framework, the Engine Platform introduces the canonical execution unit responsible for deterministic processing throughout the platform.

The Engine Foundation Platform provides standardized Engine contracts while remaining independent of lifecycle management, runtime execution, dependency management, orchestration, monitoring, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Engine Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Foundation becomes the first institutional layer of the Engine Platform.

3. Purpose

The Engine Foundation Platform answers the institutional question:

"What is an Engine?"

Rather than allowing each Engine to define its own identity and registration model, COREI introduces a standardized foundation shared by every Engine.

The Engine Foundation Platform introduces:

Engine Contract
Engine Model
Engine Registry
Engine Manager
Engine Platform Public API

It is not responsible for:

lifecycle progression
operational state
runtime execution
dependency management
orchestration
monitoring
business logic

These responsibilities remain delegated to subsequent Engine Platform layers.

4. Engine Foundation Components

The Engine Foundation Platform introduces four institutional components.

Engine Contract

Represents the canonical identity of an Engine.

Every Engine defines:

Engine Identifier
Engine Name
Engine Description

The contract establishes the institutional identity shared across all Engine implementations.

Engine Model

Represents the standardized Engine model used throughout the COREI Operating System.

The Engine Model extends the Engine Contract without introducing execution behavior.

This separation preserves flexibility for future runtime extensions while maintaining a stable identity contract.

Engine Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for all registered Engines.

Responsibilities include:

Engine registration
Engine discovery
Engine enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Engine definitions.

Unlike earlier platform implementations, the Engine Registry adopts the institutional repository pattern:

private mutable registry
exported immutable (readonly) view

This preserves encapsulation while allowing controlled registration through dedicated APIs.

Engine Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Engine Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register Engine
retrieve registered Engines
enumerate Engine definitions

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

5. Engine Foundation Model

The institutional Engine Foundation is represented as:

Engine
    │

STAGE-25 → PHASE-11 → STEP-02
Engine Lifecycle Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-02 establishes the Engine Lifecycle Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the standardized operational lifecycle of every Engine within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-01 introduced the identity and governance model of an Engine, STEP-02 defines the deterministic lifecycle through which every Engine progresses from registration to unloading.

The Engine Lifecycle Platform provides standardized lifecycle contracts while remaining independent of runtime execution, dependency management, runtime orchestration, operational monitoring, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Engine Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Lifecycle Platform becomes the second institutional layer of the Engine Platform.

3. Purpose

The Engine Lifecycle Platform answers the institutional question:

"What operational stages does an Engine progress through during its existence?"

Instead of allowing each Engine to define its own lifecycle semantics, COREI standardizes lifecycle progression across every Engine.

The Engine Lifecycle Platform introduces:

Engine Lifecycle Stage
Engine Lifecycle Contract
Default Engine Lifecycle
Engine Lifecycle Registry
Engine Lifecycle Manager
Engine Lifecycle Public API

It is not responsible for:

runtime execution
dependency resolution
runtime orchestration
operational state
health monitoring
business logic

These responsibilities remain delegated to subsequent Engine Platform layers.

4. Engine Lifecycle Components

The Engine Lifecycle Platform introduces five institutional components.

Engine Lifecycle Stage

Represents the standardized operational stages of an Engine.

The lifecycle consists of the following deterministic stages:

REGISTERED
INITIALIZING
INITIALIZED
STARTING
RUNNING
SUSPENDING
SUSPENDED
RESUMING
STOPPING
STOPPED
UNLOADING
UNLOADED
FAILED

These stages become the canonical lifecycle vocabulary for every Engine.

Engine Lifecycle Contract

Defines the canonical lifecycle interface implemented by every Engine.

The lifecycle contract standardizes:

Current Lifecycle Stage

Execution behavior remains external to the contract.

Default Engine Lifecycle

Provides the default lifecycle assigned to newly registered Engines.

Every newly created Engine begins in the:

REGISTERED

state.

Lifecycle progression occurs through controlled platform orchestration.

Engine Lifecycle Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Engine lifecycle definitions.

Responsibilities include:

lifecycle registration
lifecycle discovery
lifecycle enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Engine lifecycle definitions.

Following the institutional repository pattern introduced in PHASE-11 STEP-01, the registry exposes an immutable (readonly) view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Engine Lifecycle Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Lifecycle Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register lifecycle
retrieve lifecycle definitions
enumerate registered lifecycles

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

5. Engine Lifecycle Model

The institutional Engine Lifecycle is represented as:

REGISTERED
      │
      ▼
INITIALIZING
      │
      ▼
INITIALIZED
      │
      ▼
STARTING
      │
      ▼
RUNNING
      │
      ▼
SUSPENDING
      │
      ▼
SUSPENDED
      │
      ▼
RESUMING
      │
      ▼
RUNNING
      │
      ▼
STOPPING
      │
      ▼
STOPPED
      │
      ▼
UNLOADING
      │
      ▼
UNLOADED

Failure can occur from any operational stage:

RUNNING
      │
      ▼
FAILED

The Lifecycle Platform defines progression only.

It does not execute lifecycle transitions.

6. Relationship with the Foundation Platform

The Foundation Platform establishes Engine identity.

The Lifecycle Platform governs the operational progression of that identity.

Relationship:

Engine Foundation
        │
        ▼
Engine Lifecycle

Foundation answers:

What is this Engine?

Lifecycle answers:

What operational stage is this Engine currently in?

7. Relationship with the Future State Platform

The State Platform derives operational condition from lifecycle progression.

Relationship:

Engine Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Engine State

Lifecycle defines progression.

State defines operational condition.

Engine State Platform

1. Objective

STEP-03 establishes the Engine State Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the standardized operational state of every Engine within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-02 introduced the lifecycle progression of an Engine, STEP-03 defines its operational condition during execution.

The Engine State Platform provides standardized state contracts while remaining independent of lifecycle transitions, runtime execution, dependency management, orchestration, monitoring infrastructure, and business implementation.

COREI Operating System
        │

Engine Platform
        │

        ├── Lifecycle

The State Platform becomes the third institutional layer of the Engine Platform.

3. Purpose

The Engine State Platform answers the institutional question:

"What is the current operational condition of this Engine?"

Instead of allowing every Engine to define its own operational state model, COREI standardizes operational status and health assessment across all Engines.

The Engine State Platform introduces:

Engine Operational Status
Engine Health Status
Engine State Contract
Default Engine State
Engine State Registry
Engine State Manager
Engine State Public API

It is not responsible for:

lifecycle transitions
runtime execution
dependency resolution
runtime orchestration
infrastructure monitoring
business logic

These responsibilities remain delegated to their respective institutional platforms.

4. Engine State Components

The Engine State Platform introduces six institutional components.

Engine Operational Status

Represents the execution availability of an Engine.

The standardized operational states are:

ONLINE
OFFLINE
STARTING
STOPPING
SUSPENDED
FAILED

Operational Status reflects execution availability only.

Engine Health Status

Represents the health condition of an Engine.

The standardized health states are:

HEALTHY
DEGRADED
UNHEALTHY
UNKNOWN

Health Status represents operational quality independent of lifecycle stage.

Engine State Contract

Defines the canonical state interface implemented by every Engine.

Each Engine State contains:

Operational Status
Health Status

The contract standardizes operational state across every Engine.

Default Engine State

Provides the default operational state assigned to newly created Engines.

Every Engine begins with:

Operational Status

OFFLINE

Health Status

UNKNOWN

State values evolve during execution without modifying the foundational contract.

Engine State Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Engine state definitions.

Responsibilities include:

state registration
state discovery
state enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Engine state definitions.

Following the institutional repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Engine State Manager

Provides operational interaction with the State Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register state
retrieve state definitions
enumerate Engine states

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

5. Engine State Model

The institutional Engine State is represented as:

Engine State
        │
        ├── Operational Status
        │
        └── Health Status

The State Platform defines operational condition only.

It does not define lifecycle progression or execution.

The State Platform defines operational condition.

Relationship:

Engine Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Engine State

Lifecycle answers:

What stage is the Engine in?

State answers:

What is the operational condition of the Engine?

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

Relationship:

Engine Runtime

State provides operational condition.

Runtime provides execution representation.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-11 → STEP-04
Engine Composition Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-04 establishes the Engine Composition Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the standardized structural composition of every Engine within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-03 introduced the operational state of an Engine, STEP-04 defines how an Engine is structurally assembled from Modules.

The Engine Composition Platform provides standardized composition contracts while remaining independent of dependency management, runtime execution, orchestration, monitoring, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Engine Platform
        │

        ├── Lifecycle

3. Purpose

"Which Modules structurally belong to this Engine?"

Instead of embedding structural relationships throughout Engine implementations, COREI centralizes structural ownership within a dedicated Composition Platform.

The Engine Composition Platform introduces:

Engine Module Reference
Engine Composition Contract

Engine Composition Registry
Engine Composition Manager
Engine Composition Public API

dependency relationships

4. Engine Composition Components

The Engine Composition Platform introduces five institutional components.

Engine Module Reference

Represents a Module that structurally belongs to an Engine.

Each Module Reference defines:

Module Identifier
Module Name

Module References establish ownership only.

They do not define execution order.

Engine Composition Contract

Defines the canonical composition interface implemented by every Engine.

Each Composition contains:

Module References

The Composition Contract becomes the authoritative structural definition of an Engine.

Default Engine Composition

Provides the default structural composition assigned to newly created Engines.

Initially every Engine contains:

Modules

(empty)

Modules are introduced incrementally without modifying the foundational contract.

Engine Composition Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Engine composition definitions.

Responsibilities include:

composition registration
composition discovery
composition enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Engine composition definitions.

Following the institutional repository pattern introduced in PHASE-11 STEP-01, the Registry exposes an immutable (readonly) view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Engine Composition Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Composition Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register composition
retrieve composition definitions
enumerate Engine compositions

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

5. Engine Composition Model

The institutional Engine Composition is represented as:

Engine
    │
    ▼
Composition
    │
    ├── Module
    ├── Module
    ├── Module
    └── Module

The Composition Platform defines structural ownership only.

It does not define execution order.

6. Relationship with the Module Platform

The Module Platform defines reusable executable Modules.

The Composition Platform organizes those Modules into an Engine.

Relationship:

Module Platform
        │
        ▼
Engine Composition

Modules remain independently deployable institutional components.

The Engine Composition Platform defines which Modules belong to a specific Engine.

7. Relationship with the Dependency Platform

Composition defines structural ownership.

Dependency defines execution relationships.

Relationship:

Engine Composition
        │
        ▼
Engine Dependency

Composition answers:

Which Modules belong to this Engine?

Dependency answers:

Which Engines does this Engine depend upon?

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-11 → STEP-05
Engine Dependency Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-05 establishes the Engine Dependency Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the standardized dependency model of every Engine within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-04 introduced the structural composition of an Engine, STEP-05 defines the execution dependencies between Engines.

The Engine Dependency Platform provides standardized dependency contracts while remaining independent of runtime execution, dependency topology construction, orchestration, monitoring, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Engine Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Dependency Platform becomes the fifth institutional layer of the Engine Platform.

3. Purpose

The Engine Dependency Platform answers the institutional question:

"Which Engines does this Engine require in order to operate?"

Rather than scattering dependency declarations throughout Engine implementations, COREI centralizes dependency ownership into a dedicated institutional platform.

The Engine Dependency Platform introduces:

Engine Dependency Reference
Engine Dependency Contract
Default Engine Dependency
Engine Dependency Registry
Engine Dependency Manager
Engine Dependency Public API

It is not responsible for:

structural composition
runtime execution
dependency topology
orchestration
monitoring
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to their respective institutional platforms.

4. Engine Dependency Components

The Engine Dependency Platform introduces five institutional components.

Engine Dependency Reference

Represents another Engine required by the current Engine.

Each Dependency Reference defines:

Engine Identifier
Engine Name

Dependency References establish dependency declarations only.

They do not determine execution order.

Engine Dependency Contract

Defines the canonical dependency interface implemented by every Engine.

Each Dependency Contract contains:

Engine Dependency References

The contract becomes the authoritative dependency declaration for an Engine.

Default Engine Dependency

Provides the default dependency definition assigned to newly created Engines.

Initially every Engine contains:

Dependencies

(empty)

Dependencies are introduced incrementally without modifying the foundational contract.

Engine Dependency Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Engine dependency definitions.

Responsibilities include:

dependency registration
dependency discovery
dependency enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Engine dependency definitions.

Following the institutional repository pattern introduced in PHASE-11 STEP-01, the Registry exposes an immutable (readonly) view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Engine Dependency Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Dependency Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register dependency
retrieve dependency definitions
enumerate Engine dependencies

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

5. Engine Dependency Model

The institutional Engine Dependency model is represented as:

Engine
    │
    ▼
Dependencies
    │
    ├── Engine
    ├── Engine
    ├── Engine
    └── Engine

The Dependency Platform defines dependency declarations only.

It does not determine execution order.

6. Relationship with the Composition Platform

The Composition Platform defines structural ownership.

The Dependency Platform defines execution relationships.

Relationship:

Engine Composition
        │
        ▼
Engine Dependency

Composition answers:

Which Modules belong to this Engine?

Dependency answers:

Which Engines must be available before this Engine can execute?

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

7. Relationship with the Runtime Platform

The Runtime Platform consumes dependency definitions during Engine execution.

Relationship:

Engine Dependency
        │
        ▼
Engine Runtime

Dependency declares execution requirements.

Runtime provides the execution environment.

Engine Runtime Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-06 establishes the Engine Runtime Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the standardized runtime representation of every Engine within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-05 introduced the dependency declarations of an Engine, STEP-06 defines how an Engine exists during execution by introducing a standardized runtime model.

The Engine Runtime Platform provides standardized runtime contracts while remaining independent of dependency topology, runtime orchestration, monitoring infrastructure, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Engine Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency

        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Platform becomes the sixth institutional layer of the Engine Platform.

3. Purpose

The Engine Runtime Platform answers the institutional question:

"How is an Engine represented while it is executing?"

Instead of allowing every Engine implementation to define its own runtime representation, COREI standardizes runtime identity and execution context across the entire platform.

The Engine Runtime Platform introduces:

Engine Runtime Context
Engine Runtime Contract
Default Engine Runtime
Engine Runtime Registry
Engine Runtime Manager
Engine Runtime Public API

It is not responsible for:

lifecycle progression
dependency declarations
dependency topology
execution orchestration
monitoring
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to their respective institutional platforms.

4. Engine Runtime Components

The Engine Runtime Platform introduces five institutional components.

Engine Runtime Context

Represents the execution context of an Engine.

Each Runtime Context defines:

Runtime Identifier
Runtime Start Time

The Runtime Context provides the canonical execution identity for every Engine instance.

Engine Runtime Contract

Defines the canonical runtime interface implemented by every Engine.

Each Runtime contains:

Runtime Context

The Runtime Contract standardizes execution representation across every Engine.

Default Engine Runtime

Provides the default runtime assigned to newly created Engines.

Initially every Engine Runtime contains:

Runtime Identifier

(empty)

Runtime Start Time

Unix Epoch

Runtime values evolve during execution without modifying the foundational contract.

Engine Runtime Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Engine runtime definitions.

Responsibilities include:

runtime registration
runtime discovery
runtime enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Engine runtime definitions.

Following the institutional repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Engine Runtime Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Runtime Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register runtime
retrieve runtime definitions
enumerate Engine runtimes

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

5. Engine Runtime Model

The institutional Engine Runtime is represented as:

Engine Runtime
        │
        └── Runtime Context
                │
                ├── Runtime Identifier
                └── Runtime Start Time

The Runtime Platform defines execution representation only.

It does not coordinate execution.

The Dependency Platform defines execution prerequisites.

The Runtime Platform provides the execution representation once those prerequisites have been satisfied.

Relationship:

Engine Dependency
        │
        ▼
Engine Runtime

Dependency answers:

What must exist before execution?

Runtime answers:

How is the executing Engine represented?

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

The Integration Platform consumes the Runtime representation to establish institutional platform connectivity.

Relationship:

Engine Runtime
        │
        ▼
Engine Integration

Runtime provides execution identity.

Integration provides platform connectivity.

4. Engine Integration Components

The Engine Integration Platform introduces eight institutional components.

Engine Provider Integration

Represents Provider Platform integrations consumed by an Engine.

Typical Provider integrations include:

Database Providers
Messaging Providers
Cache Providers
Authentication Providers
Storage Providers

The Provider Integration layer standardizes infrastructure consumption.

Engine Capability Integration

Represents reusable Capabilities consumed by an Engine.

Examples include:

Logging Capability
Validation Capability
Configuration Capability
Metrics Capability
Security Capability

Capability Integration promotes institutional reuse.

Engine Service Integration

Represents Service Platform integrations consumed by an Engine.

Examples include:

Market Data Service
Portfolio Service
Risk Service
Execution Service

Services provide reusable business functionality.

Engine Module Integration

Represents Module Platform integrations consumed by an Engine.

Examples include:

Indicator Module
Signal Module
Position Module
Portfolio Module

Modules provide executable processing units.

Engine Integration Contract

Defines the canonical integration interface implemented by every Engine.

Each Integration contains:

Provider Integration
Capability Integration
Service Integration
Module Integration

The Integration Contract becomes the authoritative connectivity definition for an Engine.

Default Engine Integration

Provides the default integration definition assigned to newly created Engines.

Initially every integration collection is empty.

Platform connectivity is introduced incrementally without modifying the foundational contract.

Engine Integration Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Engine integration definitions.

Responsibilities include:

integration registration
integration discovery
integration enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Engine integration definitions.

Following the institutional repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Engine Integration Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Integration Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register integration
retrieve integration definitions
enumerate Engine integrations

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-11 → STEP-08
Engine Runtime Graph Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-08 establishes the Engine Runtime Graph Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the deterministic dependency topology of every Engine within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-07 introduced the institutional integrations consumed by an Engine, STEP-08 consolidates those integration declarations into a unified dependency graph.

The Engine Runtime Graph Platform provides standardized topology contracts while remaining independent of runtime execution, orchestration, monitoring, lifecycle management, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Engine Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Graph Platform becomes the eighth institutional layer of the Engine Platform.

3. Purpose

The Engine Runtime Graph Platform answers the institutional question:

"What is the complete dependency topology of this Engine?"

Instead of scattering dependency relationships across multiple integration layers, COREI consolidates every Engine dependency into a single authoritative Runtime Graph.

The Engine Runtime Graph Platform introduces:

Engine Runtime Node
Engine Runtime Edge
Engine Runtime Graph
Engine Runtime Graph Registry
Engine Runtime Graph Manager
Engine Runtime Graph Public API

It is not responsible for:

runtime execution
lifecycle transitions
dependency initialization
orchestration
monitoring
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to their respective institutional platforms.

4. Engine Runtime Graph Components

The Runtime Graph Platform introduces five institutional components.

Engine Runtime Node

Represents a node participating in an Engine dependency graph.

Each Runtime Node defines:

Node Identifier
Node Name

Examples include:

Engine
Provider
Capability
Service
Module

Nodes represent graph entities only.

Engine Runtime Edge

Represents a dependency relationship between two Runtime Nodes.

Each Edge defines:

Source Node
Target Node

Edges establish dependency relationships without executing them.

Engine Runtime Graph

Represents the complete dependency topology of an Engine.

Each Runtime Graph contains:

Runtime Nodes
Runtime Edges

The Runtime Graph becomes the authoritative dependency topology for that Engine.

Engine Runtime Graph Registry

Represents the centralized institutional repository for all Runtime Graph definitions.

Responsibilities include:

graph registration
graph discovery
graph enumeration

Following the institutional repository pattern, the Registry exposes an immutable (readonly) view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Engine Runtime Graph Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Runtime Graph Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register runtime graph
retrieve runtime graphs
enumerate runtime graphs

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

5. Engine Runtime Graph Model

The institutional Runtime Graph is represented as:

Engine Runtime Graph
        │
        ├── Nodes
        │
        └── Edges

The Runtime Graph defines dependency topology only.

It does not coordinate execution.

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-11 → STEP-09
Engine Runtime Orchestrator Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-09 establishes the Engine Runtime Orchestrator Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for coordinating the deterministic execution lifecycle of every Engine within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-08 introduced the Engine Runtime Graph, which models dependency topology, STEP-09 introduces the Runtime Orchestrator that consumes this topology and coordinates execution in a deterministic, repeatable, and institutionally governed manner.

The Engine Runtime Orchestrator Platform provides standardized orchestration contracts while remaining independent of dependency modeling, monitoring infrastructure, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Engine Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State

        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Orchestrator becomes the ninth and final institutional layer of the Engine Platform.

3. Purpose

The Engine Runtime Orchestrator answers the institutional question:

"How should this Engine execute according to the approved dependency topology?"

Instead of allowing each Engine to determine its own startup and shutdown behavior, COREI centralizes execution coordination into a dedicated orchestration platform.

The Engine Runtime Orchestrator Platform introduces:

Engine Runtime Orchestrator Context
Engine Runtime Orchestrator Contract
Default Engine Runtime Orchestrator
Engine Runtime Orchestrator Registry
Engine Runtime Orchestrator Manager
Engine Runtime Orchestrator Public API

dependency modeling
runtime graph construction
business logic
infrastructure monitoring
Engine implementation
