4. Engine Runtime Orchestrator Components

The Runtime Orchestrator Platform introduces six institutional components.

Engine Runtime Orchestrator Context

Represents the execution context used by the Runtime Orchestrator.

Each Context defines:

Orchestration Identifier

Future platform evolution may extend the context with:

execution session
orchestration metadata
replay context
execution policies
scheduling information

The Context provides the canonical execution identity for orchestration.

Engine Runtime Orchestrator Contract

Defines the canonical orchestration interface implemented by every Engine Runtime Orchestrator.

Each Orchestrator contains:

Runtime Orchestrator Context

The contract standardizes execution coordination across every Engine.

Default Engine Runtime Orchestrator

Provides the default orchestration definition assigned to newly created Engine orchestrators.

Initially every Runtime Orchestrator contains:

Orchestration Identifier

(empty)

Execution metadata evolves dynamically during runtime without modifying the foundational contract.

Engine Runtime Orchestrator Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Runtime Orchestrator definitions.

Responsibilities include:

orchestrator registration
orchestrator discovery
orchestrator enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Runtime Orchestrators.

Following the COREI repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Engine Runtime Orchestrator Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Runtime Orchestrator Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register orchestrator
retrieve orchestrators
enumerate orchestrators

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

Engine Runtime Orchestrator Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Runtime Orchestrator Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Runtime Orchestrator Context
Runtime Orchestrator Contract
Default Runtime Orchestrator
Registry
Manager

This becomes the official entry point for all Engine orchestration functionality.

before proceeding to phase 12 i need few clarification to confirm we are in rite track, 1. we are building kernel foundation till phase 16, 1.1 is it necessary to build from scratch, coz we installed 50+ tools any of those replace this? 1.2, give me how we are connecting all phases each other? small summary just to confirm we are doing rite .

Yes. We are on the correct track. Your question is actually one of the most important architectural validation points before continuing into PHASE-12.

1. Are we doing the right thing by building the Platform Kernel from scratch?

Yes.

But there is an important distinction.

We are not rebuilding software products.

We are building COREI's institutional operating system contracts.

That is completely different.

For example:

Docker already has:

container lifecycle
networking
storage

But Docker does not know:

what a Trading Engine is
what a Market Domain is
what a Signal Module is
what a Portfolio Service is
what a Provider means inside COREI

Only COREI can define these.

Therefore the Platform Kernel is institutional architecture, not infrastructure software.

1.1 We already installed 50+ tools. Do they replace these phases?

No.

They provide infrastructure.

The Platform Kernel provides ownership.

Think of it like this:

Infrastructure Tool	Provides	Does NOT Provide
Docker	Containers	Engine identity
Kubernetes	Scheduling	Domain ownership
Kafka	Event streaming	Event contracts for COREI
Redis	Cache	State governance
PostgreSQL	Storage	Service ownership
Vault	Secrets	Provider abstraction
Keycloak	Authentication	Capability contracts
Grafana	Dashboards	Platform intelligence model
Prometheus	Metrics	Runtime architecture
ArgoCD	Deployment	Application ownership
n8n	Automation	Institutional workflow model

The tools are the technology.

The Platform Kernel is the institutional architecture that tells those technologies how COREI is organized.

Example

Without the Platform Kernel:

Docker

↓

Container

↓

Python Process

↓

Some Code

Docker has no idea whether that code is:

Trading Engine
Risk Engine
Portfolio Engine
AI Engine

To Docker they are all identical processes.

After the Platform Kernel:

COREI Domain

↓

Engine

↓

Module

↓

Service

↓

Application

↓

Capability

↓

Provider

↓

Docker Container

↓

Kubernetes Pod

Now every infrastructure component has institutional meaning.

2. Why build from scratch?

Because COREI is not an application.

It is an Operating System.

Operating Systems always define their own abstractions.

For example:

Windows defines

Process
Thread
Handle
Device
Driver

Linux defines

Process
File
Device
Namespace
Cgroup

Neither Docker nor Kubernetes replaces these.

Similarly COREI defines

Provider
Capability
Application
Service
Module
Engine
Domain
Event
State
Extension

These are COREI's native operating system abstractions.

3. How are all phases connected?

Current Platform Kernel:

Provider
      │
      ▼
Capability
      │
      ▼
Application
      │
      ▼
Service
      │
      ▼
Module
      │
      ▼
Engine
      │
      ▼
Domain
      │
      ▼
Platform Runtime
      │
      ▼
Event
      │
      ▼
State
      │
      ▼
Extension

Every layer builds on the previous one.

Relationship Summary
PHASE-01

Provider Platform

Owns infrastructure resources.

Examples:

PostgreSQL
Redis
Kafka
Vault
Keycloak
PHASE-02

Capability Platform

Owns reusable platform capabilities.

Examples:

Logging
Validation
Configuration
Metrics
Security

Consumes Providers.

PHASE-03

Cognitive Platform

Owns the COREI cognitive workspace.

Consumes Capabilities.

PHASE-04

Workspace Foundation

Defines workspace contracts.

Consumes Cognitive Platform.

PHASE-05

Workspace Runtime

Makes workspaces executable.

Consumes Workspace Foundation.

PHASE-06

Workspace Infrastructure

Connects workspaces to infrastructure.

Consumes Runtime.

PHASE-07

Experience Platform

Groups workspaces into user experiences.

Examples:

Trading
Infrastructure
Risk
Executive

Consumes Workspace Platform.

PHASE-08

Application Platform

Defines deployable applications.

Consumes Experiences.

PHASE-09

Service Platform

Provides reusable business services.

Examples:

Portfolio Service
Risk Service
Market Data Service

Consumed by Applications.

PHASE-10

Module Platform

Provides executable functional building blocks.

Examples:

Indicator Module
Signal Module
Exposure Module
Position Module

Consumed by Services and Engines.

PHASE-11

Engine Platform

Coordinates Modules into executable processing engines.

Examples:

Market Engine
Risk Engine
Portfolio Engine
Execution Engine

Consumes Modules.

PHASE-12 (Next)

Domain Platform

Groups Engines into complete business domains.

Examples:

Market Intelligence Domain

├── Market Engine

├── Trend Engine

├── Indicator Engine

├── Volatility Engine
PHASE-13

Platform Runtime Integration

This is the bridge between the institutional kernel and the deployed runtime.

It binds Platform Kernel abstractions to the actual runtime environment, including:

Docker containers
Kubernetes pods
service discovery
dependency injection
lifecycle activation
startup sequencing
PHASE-14

Event Platform

Provides the institutional event model.

Every platform above publishes and consumes standardized events.

Examples:

Engine Started
Module Loaded
Service Registered
Domain Activated
PHASE-15

State Platform

Provides centralized institutional state ownership.

It coordinates:

global state
synchronization
persistence
replay
recovery
audit state

rather than allowing state management to become fragmented across subsystems.

PHASE-16

Extension Platform

Defines the institutional extension mechanism.

Examples include:

broker adapters
exchange adapters
AI providers
custom modules
plugins
marketplace packages

This ensures future extensibility without violating architectural boundaries.

4. Why this sequence is institutionally correct

The dependency chain is intentionally bottom-up:

Infrastructure
        │
        ▼
Providers
        │
        ▼
Capabilities
        │
        ▼
Applications
        │
        ▼
Services
        │
        ▼
Modules
        │
        ▼
Engines
        │
        ▼
Domains
        │
        ▼
Platform Runtime
        │
        ▼
Events
        │
        ▼
State
        │
        ▼
Extensions

Each layer only depends on layers beneath it, producing:

deterministic ownership
single responsibility
clear dependency direction
no circular architecture
institutional scalability

This is consistent with the architecture described in your Stage-25 continuity document and preserves the deterministic Platform Kernel that will later support the Platform Enablement phases (17–32). Based on the repository structure, approved execution model, and the completed phases through Engine Platform, the current implementation remains aligned with the intended institutional design.

STAGE-25
PHASE-12
Domain Platform

Institutional Objective

Establish the institutional Domain Platform for the COREI Operating System.

The Domain Platform introduces the canonical organizational boundary that groups multiple Engines into a coherent business capability while preserving deterministic ownership, execution isolation, lifecycle governance, dependency management, runtime coordination, and architectural symmetry.

Unlike the Engine Platform, which manages executable processing units, the Domain Platform manages collections of Engines that together deliver a complete institutional business function.

Domain Platform Responsibilities

The Domain Platform owns:

Domain identity
Domain lifecycle
Domain operational state
Domain composition
Domain dependency declarations
Domain runtime representation
Domain platform integrations
Domain runtime topology
Domain runtime orchestration

The Domain Platform does not own:

business logic
Engine implementation
Module implementation
Service implementation
Provider implementation
Institutional Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Domain Platform
        │
        ├── Engine A
        ├── Engine B
        ├── Engine C
        └── Engine D
Domain Ownership

A Domain owns a complete business capability.

Examples include:

Market Intelligence Domain

Risk Intelligence Domain

Portfolio Intelligence Domain

Execution Intelligence Domain

Performance Intelligence Domain

Infrastructure Intelligence Domain

Research Intelligence Domain

Compliance Intelligence Domain

Each Domain becomes an independent execution boundary.

Relationship
Provider
      │
Capability
      │
Application
      │
Service
      │
Module
      │
Engine
      │
Domain

Domains consume Engines.

Engines never own Domains.

PHASE-12 Implementation Lifecycle

Following the institutional symmetry established across PHASE-01 through PHASE-11, the Domain Platform consists of nine implementation steps.

STEP	Platform Layer	Purpose
STEP-01	Domain Foundation	Establish Domain identity, contracts, registry, manager, models, and public API
STEP-02	Domain Lifecycle	Standardize Domain lifecycle stages and lifecycle management
STEP-03	Domain State	Standardize Domain operational state and health management
STEP-04	Domain Composition	Define structural composition of Domains through Engine references
STEP-05	Domain Dependency	Declare deterministic Domain-to-Domain dependencies
STEP-06	Domain Runtime	Define runtime representation for executing Domains
STEP-07	Domain Integration	Integrate Domains with Provider, Capability, Service, Module, and Engine platforms
STEP-08	Domain Runtime Graph	Consolidate Domain dependencies into a deterministic execution topology
STEP-09	Domain Runtime Orchestrator	Coordinate deterministic Domain execution over the Runtime Graph
Expected Repository Structure
src/domains/

contracts/
models/

registry/
manager/

lifecycle/
state/
composition/
dependency/
runtime/
integration/
runtime-graph/
runtime-orchestrator/

index.ts
Institutional Domain Model
Domain
      │
      ├── Engine
      ├── Engine
      ├── Engine
      ├── Engine
      └── Engine

Example:

Market Intelligence Domain

├── Market Data Engine
├── Market Health Engine
├── Technical Indicator Engine
├── Trend Analysis Engine
├── Volatility Analysis Engine
├── Momentum Analysis Engine
├── Market Structure Engine
├── Market Regime Engine
└── Market Intelligence State Engine
Position within the Platform Kernel
Provider
      │
Capability
      │
Application
      │
Service
      │
Module
      │
Engine
      │
Domain
      │
Platform Runtime Integration
      │
Event
      │
State
      │
Extension

The Domain Platform completes the institutional business hierarchy by introducing the first layer that represents complete business capabilities rather than individual execution components.

Deliverables

Upon completion of PHASE-12, the COREI Operating System will provide:

Standardized Domain identity
Domain lifecycle management
Domain operational state management
Engine composition within Domains
Domain dependency declarations
Domain runtime representation
Institutional platform integrations
Deterministic Domain dependency topology
Deterministic Domain runtime orchestration
Preparation for PHASE-13

With the Domain Platform complete, the Platform Kernel transitions from structural platforms to cross-platform coordination.

PHASE-13 – Platform Runtime Integration will unify all foundational platforms (Provider, Capability, Application, Service, Module, Engine, and Domain) into a single institutional runtime, enabling deterministic initialization, dependency resolution, activation sequencing, runtime coordination, and platform-wide execution governance. This phase forms the bridge between the Platform Kernel and the operational runtime of the COREI Operating System.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-12 → STEP-01
Domain Foundation Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-01 establishes the Domain Foundation Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the canonical identity and ownership model of every Domain within the COREI Operating System.

While the Engine Platform defines executable processing units, the Domain Platform introduces the first business-level execution boundary that groups multiple Engines into a complete institutional capability.

The Domain Foundation Platform provides standardized Domain identity contracts while remaining independent of lifecycle management, operational state, structural composition, dependency management, runtime execution, platform integrations, dependency topology, orchestration, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Domain Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Foundation Platform becomes the first institutional layer of the Domain Platform.

3. Purpose

The Domain Foundation Platform answers the institutional question:

"What is a Domain within the COREI Operating System?"

Instead of allowing every business area to define its own identity model, COREI standardizes Domain ownership through a dedicated foundational platform.

The Domain Foundation Platform introduces:

Domain Contract
Domain Model
Domain Registry
Domain Manager
Domain Public API

It is not responsible for:

lifecycle transitions
runtime execution
Engine composition
dependency declarations
platform integrations
orchestration
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to their respective institutional platforms.

4. Domain Foundation Components

The Domain Foundation Platform introduces five institutional components.

Domain Contract

Represents the canonical identity of a Domain.

Each Domain defines:

Domain Identifier
Domain Name
Domain Description

The contract establishes the institutional identity shared by every Domain.

Domain Model

Represents the standardized implementation model of a Domain.

The Domain Model provides a consistent representation of the Domain Contract throughout the COREI Operating System.

Future enhancements may extend the model while preserving the foundational contract.

Domain Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Domain definitions.

Responsibilities include:

Domain registration
Domain discovery
Domain enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of every Domain defined within the platform.

Following the institutional repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Domain Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Domain Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register Domain
retrieve Domain definitions
enumerate Domains

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

Domain Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Domain Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Contracts
Models
Registry
Manager

This becomes the official entry point for every Domain within COREI.

5. Domain Foundation Model

The institutional Domain Foundation is represented as:

Domain Platform
        │
        ├── Contracts
        ├── Models
        ├── Registry
        └── Manager

Each Domain is represented as:

Domain

├── Identifier

├── Name

└── Description

The Foundation Platform establishes Domain identity only.

It does not define operational behavior.

6. Relationship with the Engine Platform

The Engine Platform defines executable processing units.

The Domain Platform organizes those Engines into complete business capabilities.

Relationship:

Engine Platform
        │
        ▼
Domain Platform

Engines answer:

What processing unit performs work?

Domains answer:

Which Engines together provide a complete institutional capability?

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

      │

Runtime Orchestrator

Every subsequent layer extends the foundational identity established during STEP-01.

Domain Lifecycle Platform

1. Objective

STEP-02 establishes the Domain Lifecycle Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the standardized lifecycle progression of every Domain within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-01 introduced the canonical identity of a Domain, STEP-02 defines how every Domain progresses through its operational existence using a deterministic lifecycle model.

The Domain Lifecycle Platform provides standardized lifecycle contracts while remaining independent of operational state, structural composition, dependency management, runtime execution, platform integration, dependency topology, orchestration, monitoring infrastructure, and business implementation.

3. Purpose

"How does a Domain progress throughout its operational lifetime?"

Rather than allowing every Domain to define its own operational progression, COREI standardizes lifecycle management through a dedicated institutional platform.

4. Domain Lifecycle Components

The Domain Lifecycle Platform introduces six institutional components.

Domain Lifecycle Stage

Represents the standardized operational progression of a Domain.

The approved lifecycle stages are:

CREATED
REGISTERED
INITIALIZED
READY
RUNNING
SUSPENDED
STOPPED
TERMINATED

Every Domain progresses through these stages in a deterministic manner.

Domain Lifecycle Contract

Defines the canonical lifecycle interface implemented by every Domain.

Each Lifecycle contains:

Lifecycle Stage

The Lifecycle Contract standardizes operational progression across every Domain.

Default Domain Lifecycle

Provides the default lifecycle assigned to newly created Domains.

Initially every Domain begins in:

CREATED

The lifecycle progresses through later stages without modifying the foundational contract.

Domain Lifecycle Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Domain lifecycle definitions.

Responsibilities include:

lifecycle registration
lifecycle discovery
lifecycle enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Domain lifecycle definitions.

Following the institutional repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Domain Lifecycle Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Lifecycle Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register lifecycle
retrieve lifecycle definitions
enumerate Domain lifecycles

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

Domain Lifecycle Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Lifecycle Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Lifecycle Stage
Lifecycle Contract
Default Lifecycle
Registry
Manager

This becomes the official entry point for Domain lifecycle functionality.

5. Lifecycle Model

The institutional Domain Lifecycle is represented as:

CREATED
     │
REGISTERED
     │
INITIALIZED
     │
READY
     │
RUNNING
     │
SUSPENDED
     │
STOPPED
     │
TERMINATED

The Lifecycle Platform defines operational progression only.

It does not execute the Domain.

The Foundation Platform defines Domain identity.

The Lifecycle Platform defines operational progression.

Relationship:

Domain Foundation
        │
        ▼
Domain Lifecycle

Foundation answers:

What is this Domain?

Lifecycle answers:

What operational stage is the Domain currently in?

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

The State Platform consumes lifecycle information to determine the operational condition of the Domain.

Relationship:

Domain Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Domain State

Lifecycle describes progression.

State describes operational condition.

4. Domain State Components

Domain Health Status

Represents the standardized health classification of a Domain.

The approved health states are:

UNKNOWN
HEALTHY
DEGRADED
UNHEALTHY

Every Domain reports its operational condition using this standardized classification.

Domain State Contract

Defines the canonical operational state interface implemented by every Domain.

Each Domain State contains:

Enabled
Healthy
Health Status

The State Contract standardizes operational condition reporting across every Domain.

Default Domain State

Provides the default operational state assigned to newly created Domains.

Initially every Domain begins with:

Enabled      : false
Healthy      : false
Health       : UNKNOWN

The operational condition evolves dynamically while preserving the foundational contract.

Domain State Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Domain state definitions.

Responsibilities include:

state registration
state discovery
state enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Domain state definitions.

Following the institutional repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Domain State Manager

Domain Composition Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-04 establishes the Domain Composition Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the structural composition of every Domain within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-03 introduced the operational state of a Domain, STEP-04 defines which Engines constitute that Domain and how they are organized into a single institutional business capability.

The Domain Composition Platform provides standardized composition contracts while remaining independent of dependency declarations, runtime execution, platform integration, dependency topology, orchestration, monitoring infrastructure, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Domain Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Composition Platform becomes the fourth institutional layer of the Domain Platform.

3. Purpose

The Domain Composition Platform answers the institutional question:

"Which Engines structurally belong to this Domain?"

Rather than allowing Engine ownership to become distributed across implementations, COREI centralizes structural ownership within the Composition Platform.

The Domain Composition Platform introduces:

Domain Engine Reference
Domain Composition Contract
Default Domain Composition
Domain Composition Registry
Domain Composition Manager
Domain Composition Public API

It is not responsible for:

dependency declarations
runtime execution
execution ordering
platform integration
runtime topology
orchestration
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to later institutional platforms.

4. Domain Composition Components

The Domain Composition Platform introduces six institutional components.

Domain Engine Reference

Represents the canonical reference to an Engine belonging to a Domain.

Each reference defines:

Engine Identifier

The Engine Reference standardizes Engine membership across all Domains.

Domain Composition Contract

Defines the canonical structural composition interface implemented by every Domain.

Each Composition contains:

Engine References

The Composition Contract standardizes Domain structure throughout the COREI Operating System.

Default Domain Composition

Provides the default structural composition assigned to newly created Domains.

Initially every Domain contains:

Engines

(empty)

Engine membership is introduced incrementally without modifying the foundational contract.

Domain Composition Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Domain composition definitions.

Responsibilities include:

composition registration
composition discovery
composition enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Domain structural definitions.

Following the COREI repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Domain Composition Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Composition Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register composition
retrieve composition definitions
enumerate Domain compositions

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

Domain Composition Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Composition Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Engine Reference
Composition Contract
Default Composition
Registry
Manager

This becomes the official entry point for Domain structural composition.

Market Intelligence Domain
        │

        ├── Trend Analysis Engine
        ├── Volatility Analysis Engine
        ├── Momentum Analysis Engine
        ├── Market Structure Engine
        ├── Market Regime Engine
        └── Market Intelligence State Engine

The Composition Platform defines structural ownership only.

It does not define dependency relationships.

The Composition Platform defines the Engines that constitute the Domain.

Domain State

Domain Composition

Dependency establishes institutional relationships.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-12 → STEP-07
Domain Integration Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-07 establishes the Domain Integration Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the standardized integration model between the Domain Platform and the underlying COREI Platform Kernel.

While STEP-06 introduced the runtime representation of a Domain, STEP-07 establishes how Domains connect to Providers, Capabilities, Services, Modules, and Engines without assuming ownership of those platforms.

The Domain Integration Platform provides standardized integration contracts while remaining independent of runtime topology, execution orchestration, monitoring infrastructure, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Domain Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Integration Platform becomes the seventh institutional layer of the Domain Platform.

3. Purpose

The Domain Integration Platform answers the institutional question:

"Which institutional platform components are consumed by this Domain?"

Instead of embedding platform connectivity throughout Domain implementations, COREI centralizes integration declarations into a dedicated institutional platform.

The Domain Integration Platform introduces:

Domain Provider Integration
Domain Capability Integration
Domain Service Integration
Domain Module Integration
Domain Engine Integration
Domain Integration Contract
Default Domain Integration
Domain Integration Registry
Domain Integration Manager
Domain Integration Public API

It is not responsible for:

dependency topology
execution sequencing
runtime orchestration
monitoring
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to later institutional platforms.

4. Domain Integration Components

The Domain Integration Platform introduces ten institutional components.

Domain Provider Integration

Represents the infrastructure Providers consumed by a Domain.

Examples include:

PostgreSQL Provider
Kafka Provider
Redis Provider
Vault Provider

The contract standardizes infrastructure connectivity.

Domain Capability Integration

Represents reusable Capabilities consumed by a Domain.

Examples include:

Logging
Configuration
Validation
Metrics
Security

The contract standardizes reusable platform capabilities.

Domain Service Integration

Represents Services consumed by a Domain.

Examples include:

Portfolio Service
Market Data Service
Risk Service

The contract standardizes service consumption.

Domain Module Integration

Represents Modules consumed by a Domain.

Examples include:

Indicator Module
Trend Module
Signal Module
Exposure Module

The contract standardizes Module integration.

Domain Engine Integration

Represents Engines referenced by the Domain.

Examples include:

Market Engine
Risk Engine
Execution Engine
Portfolio Engine

The contract standardizes Engine integration.

Domain Integration Contract

Provides the canonical integration model for every Domain.

Each Domain Integration contains:

Provider Integration
Capability Integration
Service Integration
Module Integration
Engine Integration

The contract becomes the authoritative integration model for every Domain.

Default Domain Integration

Provides the default integration definition assigned to newly created Domains.

Initially every integration collection is empty.

Integrations are added progressively while preserving the foundational contract.

Domain Integration Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Domain integration definitions.

Responsibilities include:

integration registration
integration discovery
integration enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository of Domain integration definitions.

Following the COREI repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Domain Integration Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Integration Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register integration
retrieve integration definitions
enumerate Domain integrations

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

Domain Integration Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Integration Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Provider Integration
Capability Integration
Service Integration
Module Integration
Engine Integration
Domain Integration
Default Integration
Registry
Manager

This becomes the official entry point for Domain integration functionality.

5. Integration Model

The institutional Domain Integration model is represented as:

Domain

├── Provider Integration
├── Capability Integration
├── Service Integration
├── Module Integration
└── Engine Integration

The Integration Platform defines institutional connectivity only.

It does not perform dependency resolution or execution.

6. Relationship with the Runtime Platform

The Runtime Platform defines executable representation.

The Integration Platform defines institutional connectivity.

Relationship:

Domain Runtime
        │
        ▼
Domain Integration

Runtime answers:

How is the Domain represented during execution?

Integration answers:

Which COREI platform components does the Domain consume?

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

7. Relationship with the Runtime Graph Platform

The Runtime Graph Platform consumes integration declarations while constructing deterministic execution topology.

Relationship:

Domain Integration
        │
        ▼
Domain Runtime Graph

Integration establishes institutional connectivity.

Runtime Graph transforms that connectivity into deterministic topology.

Domain Runtime Graph Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-08 establishes the Domain Runtime Graph Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the deterministic dependency topology of every Domain within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-07 introduced standardized platform integration declarations, STEP-08 consolidates those declarations together with Domain dependency relationships into a single authoritative Runtime Graph.

The Domain Runtime Graph Platform provides standardized topology contracts while remaining independent of runtime execution, orchestration, monitoring infrastructure, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Domain Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle

        └── Runtime Orchestrator

3. Purpose

The Domain Runtime Graph Platform answers the institutional question:

"What is the complete dependency topology of this Domain?"

Rather than leaving dependency relationships distributed across multiple integration layers, COREI consolidates them into a centralized Runtime Graph.

The Domain Runtime Graph Platform introduces:

Domain Runtime Node
Domain Runtime Edge
Domain Runtime Graph
Domain Runtime Graph Registry
Domain Runtime Graph Public API

It is not responsible for:

runtime execution
dependency initialization
execution sequencing
lifecycle transitions
orchestration
monitoring
business logic

These responsibilities remain delegated to the Runtime Platform and the Runtime Orchestrator.

4. Domain Runtime Graph Components

The Runtime Graph Platform introduces five institutional components.

Domain Runtime Node

Represents a node participating in the Domain dependency graph.

Each Runtime Node defines:

Node Identifier
Node Name

Examples include:

Domain
Provider
Capability
Service
Module
Engine

Nodes represent graph entities only.

Domain Runtime Edge

Represents a dependency relationship between two Runtime Nodes.

Each edge defines:

Source Node
Target Node

Edges establish dependency relationships without executing them.

Domain Runtime Graph

Represents the complete dependency topology of a Domain.

Each Runtime Graph contains:

Runtime Nodes
Runtime Edges

The graph becomes the authoritative dependency topology for that Domain.

Domain Runtime Graph Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository of Runtime Graph definitions.

Responsibilities include:

graph registration
graph discovery
graph enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository for Domain Runtime Graphs.

Domain Runtime Graph Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Runtime Graph Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Runtime Node
Runtime Edge
Runtime Graph
Runtime Graph Registry

This becomes the official entry point for Domain dependency topology.

5. Runtime Graph Model

The institutional Runtime Graph is represented as:

Domain Runtime Graph
        │
        ├── Nodes
        │
        └── Edges

The Runtime Graph consolidates all dependency declarations into a deterministic topology.

The Integration Platform defines institutional connectivity.

The Runtime Graph Platform transforms those declarations into dependency topology.

Relationship:

Domain Integration
        │
        ▼
Domain Runtime Graph

Which platform components are connected?

Runtime Graph answers:

How are those components connected?

Relationship:

        │

The Runtime Orchestrator performs deterministic scheduling.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-12 → STEP-09
Domain Runtime Orchestrator Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-09 establishes the Domain Runtime Orchestrator Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for coordinating the deterministic execution lifecycle of every Domain within the COREI Operating System.

While STEP-08 introduced the Runtime Graph that models the complete dependency topology of a Domain, STEP-09 introduces the Runtime Orchestrator that consumes that topology and coordinates execution without owning dependency modeling.

The Domain Runtime Orchestrator Platform provides standardized orchestration contracts while remaining independent of dependency declarations, runtime representation, monitoring infrastructure, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Domain Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Orchestrator becomes the ninth institutional layer of the Domain Platform.

3. Purpose

The Domain Runtime Orchestrator answers the institutional question:

"How is this Domain executed in a deterministic and coordinated manner?"

Instead of allowing execution sequencing to become distributed throughout implementations, COREI centralizes execution coordination within a dedicated orchestration platform.

The Domain Runtime Orchestrator introduces:

Domain Runtime Orchestrator
Domain Runtime Orchestrator Registry
Domain Runtime Orchestrator Manager
Domain Runtime Orchestrator Public API

It is not responsible for:

dependency declarations
runtime topology
lifecycle definitions
monitoring
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to the Runtime Graph, Lifecycle Platform, and Monitoring Platform.

4. Domain Runtime Orchestrator Components

The Runtime Orchestrator Platform introduces four institutional components.

Domain Runtime Orchestrator

Represents the canonical execution coordinator for a Domain.

Each Runtime Orchestrator defines:

Orchestrator Identifier
Orchestrator Name

Future platform evolution may extend the orchestrator with:

startup planning
dependency scheduling
execution policies
recovery strategies
replay coordination
distributed execution

The Runtime Orchestrator becomes the authoritative execution coordinator for every Domain.

Domain Runtime Orchestrator Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Runtime Orchestrators.

Responsibilities include:

orchestrator registration
orchestrator discovery
orchestrator enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository for Domain Runtime Orchestrators.

Following the COREI repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Domain Runtime Orchestrator Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Runtime Orchestrator Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register orchestrator
retrieve orchestrators
enumerate orchestrators

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

Domain Runtime Orchestrator Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Runtime Orchestrator Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Runtime Orchestrator
Registry
Manager

This becomes the official entry point for Domain execution orchestration.

          ├── Initialize

          ├── Stop
          └── Shutdown

The Runtime Orchestrator coordinates execution only.

It does not define dependency topology.

The Runtime Orchestrator coordinates execution using that topology.

Relationship:

Domain Runtime Graph
          │
          ▼
Domain Runtime Orchestrator

What must execute?

In what deterministic order should execution occur?

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

Platform Runtime Integration coordinates execution across all COREI platforms.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-13 → STEP-01
Platform Runtime Foundation

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-01 establishes the Platform Runtime Foundation, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the canonical runtime identity of the COREI Operating System.

Unlike the previous Platform Kernel phases, which established independent platforms, PHASE-13 begins constructing the unified execution layer that coordinates every completed platform into a single institutional runtime.

The Platform Runtime Foundation introduces the foundational contracts, models, registries, managers, and public APIs that become the identity layer for the Platform Runtime Integration Platform.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Platform Runtime Integration
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Foundation becomes the first institutional layer of the Platform Runtime Integration Platform.

3. Purpose

The Platform Runtime Foundation answers the institutional question:

"What is the canonical identity of the unified COREI Platform Runtime?"

Instead of creating another independent platform, this phase introduces the foundational identity that represents the entire Platform Runtime Integration layer.

The Platform Runtime Foundation introduces:

Platform Runtime Contract
Platform Runtime Model
Platform Runtime Registry
Platform Runtime Manager
Platform Runtime Public API

It is not responsible for:

lifecycle progression
operational state
platform composition
dependency management
runtime execution
platform integration
dependency topology
orchestration
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to the subsequent steps of PHASE-13.

4. Platform Runtime Foundation Components

The Platform Runtime Foundation introduces five institutional components.

Platform Runtime Contract

Represents the canonical identity contract of the Platform Runtime.

Each Platform Runtime defines:

Runtime Identifier
Runtime Name
Runtime Version

This contract establishes the institutional identity of the unified runtime.

Platform Runtime Model

Provides the canonical implementation model of the Platform Runtime.

The Runtime Model extends the Runtime Contract and becomes the authoritative representation used throughout the Platform Runtime Integration Platform.

Platform Runtime Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Platform Runtime definitions.

Responsibilities include:

runtime registration
runtime discovery
runtime enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository for Platform Runtime instances.

Following the COREI repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Platform Runtime Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Platform Runtime Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register runtime
retrieve runtime definitions
enumerate platform runtimes

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

Platform Runtime Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Platform Runtime Foundation.

The Public API exposes:

Runtime Contract
Runtime Model
Runtime Registry
Runtime Manager

This becomes the official entry point for the Platform Runtime Integration Platform.

The institutional Platform Runtime Foundation is represented as:

     │
Model
     │
Registry
     │
Manager

The Foundation defines institutional identity only.

No execution behavior is introduced at this stage.

STEP-02 establishes the Platform Runtime Lifecycle, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the deterministic lifecycle progression of the unified COREI Platform Runtime.

While STEP-01 introduced the canonical identity of the Platform Runtime Integration Platform, STEP-02 defines how that runtime progresses from creation through initialization, execution, suspension, shutdown, and eventual destruction.

The Platform Runtime Lifecycle provides standardized lifecycle contracts while remaining independent of operational state, structural composition, dependency management, runtime execution, integration, dependency topology, orchestration, monitoring, and business implementation.

Platform Runtime Lifecycle Status

STARTING

SUSPENDED
STOPPING
STOPPED
DESTROYED

Every Platform Runtime progresses through this deterministic lifecycle.

Platform Runtime Lifecycle Contract

Defines the canonical lifecycle interface implemented by every Platform Runtime.

Each lifecycle contains:

Lifecycle Status

The Lifecycle Contract standardizes lifecycle progression across the entire COREI Operating System.

Default Platform Runtime Lifecycle

Provides the default lifecycle assigned to newly created Platform Runtime instances.

Initially every Platform Runtime begins as:

Status

CREATED

The lifecycle evolves deterministically while preserving the standardized contract.

Platform Runtime Lifecycle Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Platform Runtime lifecycle definitions.

Responsibilities include:

lifecycle registration
lifecycle discovery
lifecycle enumeration

Responsibilities include:

Platform Runtime Lifecycle Public API

Platform Runtime State

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-03 establishes the Platform Runtime State, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the standardized operational condition of the unified COREI Platform Runtime.

While STEP-02 introduced deterministic lifecycle progression, STEP-03 defines the operational health of the Platform Runtime independently of its lifecycle stage. This separation enables the COREI Operating System to distinguish where the runtime is in its execution lifecycle from how well it is currently operating.

The Platform Runtime State provides standardized operational state contracts while remaining independent of composition, dependency modeling, runtime execution, platform integration, runtime topology, orchestration, monitoring implementation, and business logic.

        │

3. Purpose

The Platform Runtime State answers the institutional question:

"What is the current operational condition of the unified COREI Platform Runtime?"

Lifecycle determines execution progression.

State determines operational condition.

A runtime may be:

RUNNING + HEALTHY
RUNNING + DEGRADED
RUNNING + WARNING
RUNNING + FAILED
STOPPING + HEALTHY

Lifecycle and State therefore remain completely independent.

Platform Runtime State Registry

Platform Runtime State Public API

It does not own:

lifecycle progression
runtime execution
dependency topology
orchestration
business implementation

Those responsibilities remain delegated to other Platform Runtime layers.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-13 → STEP-04
Platform Runtime Composition

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-04 establishes the Platform Runtime Composition, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the structural composition of the unified COREI Platform Runtime.

While STEP-03 introduced the operational state of the Platform Runtime, STEP-04 defines what the unified runtime is structurally composed of by assembling every completed Platform Kernel component into a single deterministic runtime composition.

The Platform Runtime Composition provides standardized structural composition contracts while remaining independent of dependency topology, runtime execution, platform integration, orchestration, monitoring implementation, and business logic.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Platform Runtime Integration
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Composition Platform becomes the fourth institutional layer of the Platform Runtime Integration Platform.

3. Purpose

The Platform Runtime Composition answers the institutional question:

"Which completed platforms collectively form the unified COREI Platform Runtime?"

Instead of allowing platform relationships to remain scattered throughout the kernel, COREI centralizes structural composition into a dedicated institutional platform.

The Platform Runtime Composition introduces:

Platform Runtime Component
Platform Runtime Composition
Default Platform Runtime Composition
Platform Runtime Composition Registry
Platform Runtime Composition Manager
Platform Runtime Composition Public API

It is not responsible for:

dependency relationships
execution sequencing
runtime orchestration
monitoring
business implementation

Those responsibilities remain delegated to subsequent Platform Runtime Integration layers.

4. Platform Runtime Composition Components

The Platform Runtime Composition introduces six institutional components.

Platform Runtime Component

Represents a single completed platform participating in the unified Platform Runtime.

Examples include:

Provider Platform
Capability Platform
Cognitive Platform
Workspace Platform
Experience Platform
Application Platform
Service Platform
Module Platform
Engine Platform
Domain Platform

Each Runtime Component becomes a structural building block of the unified runtime.

Platform Runtime Composition

Each Runtime Composition contains:

Runtime Components

The Composition Contract becomes the authoritative structural representation of the unified runtime.

Default Platform Runtime Composition

Provides the default structural composition assigned to newly created Platform Runtime instances.

Initially every composition contains:

Components

(empty)

Components are added progressively while preserving the standardized contract.

Platform Runtime Composition Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Platform Runtime composition definitions.

Responsibilities include:

composition registration
composition discovery
composition enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository for Platform Runtime composition definitions.

Following the COREI repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Platform Runtime Composition Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Composition Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register composition
retrieve composition definitions
enumerate runtime compositions

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

Platform Runtime Composition Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Composition Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Runtime Component
Runtime Composition
Default Composition
Registry
Manager

This becomes the official entry point for Platform Runtime composition functionality.

Composition defines structure.
Dependency defines relationships.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-13 → STEP-06
Platform Runtime Execution Platform

Status: COMPLETED (Revised Institutional Architecture)

1. Objective

STEP-06 establishes the Platform Runtime Execution Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for executing the unified COREI Platform Runtime.

Unlike the original implementation, which incorrectly redefined the Platform Runtime identity, the revised architecture strictly separates runtime identity from runtime execution.

This follows one of the COREI Kernel Laws:

Identity and Execution are independent institutional responsibilities.

The Runtime Execution Platform therefore consumes the Platform Runtime Foundation rather than redefining it.

2. Architectural Correction

During implementation, an architectural conflict was discovered.

The original implementation introduced:

PlatformRuntime
PlatformRuntimeRegistry
PlatformRuntimeManager

inside the Runtime layer.

However, these components had already been established during STEP-01 (Foundation).

This created duplicate exports such as:

PlatformRuntime

PlatformRuntimeRegistry

PlatformRuntimeManager

TypeScript correctly rejected the architecture because two different institutional layers attempted to own the same contracts.

Following the COREI No Patch Work policy, the Runtime layer was redesigned from first principles rather than patched.

3. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Platform Runtime Integration
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Runtime Execution
        ├── Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Execution Platform becomes the sixth institutional layer.

4. Purpose

The Runtime Execution Platform answers the institutional question:

"How is the Platform Runtime executed?"

Previous layers define:

identity
lifecycle
health
composition
dependency

STEP-06 introduces:

execution
scheduling
dispatching
execution context

without redefining runtime identity.

5. Runtime Execution Components

The revised Runtime Platform introduces five institutional components.

Platform Runtime Executor

Represents the canonical execution interface for the Platform Runtime.

Responsibilities include:

initiating execution
consuming Platform Runtime models
providing deterministic execution entry

The Executor becomes the institutional execution gateway.

Platform Runtime Execution Context

Represents the execution environment for the Platform Runtime.

Each execution context contains:

Platform Runtime
execution timestamp

Future versions may extend the context with:

execution identifiers
runtime metadata
security context
tracing information
execution policies

The Execution Context encapsulates runtime execution information.

Platform Runtime Dispatcher

Coordinates execution dispatch.

Responsibilities include:

dispatch runtime
forward execution requests
isolate execution routing

The Dispatcher owns execution distribution.

Platform Runtime Scheduler

Coordinates execution scheduling.

Responsibilities include:

scheduling runtime execution
execution timing
startup sequencing
deferred execution

The Scheduler owns execution timing.

Platform Runtime Service

Aggregates the Runtime Execution Platform.

Each Runtime Service contains:

Executor
Dispatcher
Scheduler

The Runtime Service becomes the operational entry point for runtime execution.

6. Runtime Execution Model

The institutional Runtime Execution Platform is represented as:

Platform Runtime

        │

Execution Context

        │

Runtime Executor

        │

Dispatcher

        │

Scheduler

Execution services consume the Platform Runtime.

They do not redefine it.

        │

15. Architectural Recovery

During STEP-06 implementation, an architectural collision between the Foundation and Runtime layers was identified.

Rather than renaming duplicate symbols or applying incremental fixes, the Runtime layer was redesigned to align with the COREI institutional ownership model.

The revised design ensures:

Foundation exclusively owns runtime identity.
Runtime exclusively owns execution services.
Export ambiguity is eliminated.
Layer responsibilities remain orthogonal.
Future extensions can evolve without ownership conflicts.

This corrective action follows the COREI No Patch Work engineering policy and preserves long-term architectural integrity.

Platform Runtime Execution Plan

execution identifier
execution name

Platform Runtime Startup Strategy

Represents the deterministic startup policy for the Platform Runtime.

Responsibilities include:

startup sequencing
initialization coordination

The Startup Strategy defines how runtime begins.

Platform Runtime Shutdown Strategy

Represents the deterministic shutdown policy.

Responsibilities include:

graceful shutdown
dependency-aware termination
cleanup coordination

The Shutdown Strategy defines how runtime ends.

Platform Runtime Coordination Service

Coordinates execution using the Runtime Graph and Execution Plan.

Responsibilities include:

runtime coordination
synchronization
execution management
runtime consistency

The Coordination Service becomes the operational coordinator for runtime execution.

Platform Runtime Orchestrator

Represents the institutional orchestration interface.

Execution interface

STAGE-25 → PHASE-14 → STEP-01
Platform Bootstrap Foundation

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-01 establishes the Platform Bootstrap Foundation, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the canonical identity of the Platform Bootstrap Platform.

Following the completion of PHASE-13 (Platform Runtime Integration), the COREI Operating System possesses a complete deterministic runtime. The responsibility of PHASE-14 is to transform that runtime into an operational platform capable of deterministic startup.

The Foundation layer introduces the identity of the Platform Bootstrap Platform while remaining independent of lifecycle management, operational state, structural composition, dependency management, execution, validation, pipeline processing, orchestration, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Platform Bootstrap
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Execution
        ├── Validation
        ├── Pipeline
        └── Orchestrator

The Foundation becomes the first institutional layer of the Platform Bootstrap Platform.

3. Purpose

The Platform Bootstrap Foundation answers the institutional question:

"What is the Platform Bootstrap?"

It establishes the canonical identity of the bootstrap subsystem before introducing lifecycle progression, execution sequencing, dependency validation, or orchestration.

STEP-01 introduces:

Platform Bootstrap Contract
Platform Bootstrap Model
Platform Bootstrap Registry
Platform Bootstrap Manager
Platform Bootstrap Public API

It is not responsible for:

startup lifecycle
bootstrap execution
dependency validation
readiness verification
orchestration
business implementation

Those responsibilities remain delegated to the subsequent bootstrap layers.

4. Platform Bootstrap Foundation Components

The Foundation introduces four institutional components.

Platform Bootstrap Contract

Represents the canonical identity of the Platform Bootstrap.

Each Platform Bootstrap defines:

Bootstrap Identifier
Bootstrap Name

This contract becomes the authoritative identity definition for the bootstrap subsystem.

Platform Bootstrap Model

Represents the institutional implementation model of the Platform Bootstrap.

The model extends the Platform Bootstrap contract while preserving a clear separation between contractual identity and implementation.

Future bootstrap metadata can be added to the model without modifying the public contract.

Platform Bootstrap Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Platform Bootstrap definitions.

Responsibilities include:

bootstrap registration
bootstrap discovery
bootstrap enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository for Platform Bootstrap definitions.

Following the COREI repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Platform Bootstrap Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Bootstrap Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register bootstrap
retrieve bootstrap definitions
enumerate bootstrap instances

Operational management remains isolated from repository storage.

Platform Bootstrap Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Foundation Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Platform Bootstrap Contract
Platform Bootstrap Model
Platform Bootstrap Registry
Platform Bootstrap Manager

This becomes the official entry point for Platform Bootstrap functionality.

5. Foundation Model

The institutional Platform Bootstrap Foundation is represented as:

Platform Bootstrap

        │

PlatformBootstrap

        │

PlatformBootstrapModel

        │

PlatformBootstrapRegistry

        │

PlatformBootstrapManager

The Foundation establishes identity only.

It does not define startup behavior.

4. Platform Bootstrap Lifecycle Components

Platform Bootstrap Lifecycle Status

Represents the deterministic lifecycle states of the Platform Bootstrap.

The lifecycle defines the following institutional states:

CREATED

INITIALIZING

VALIDATING

READY

FAILED

Each state represents a distinct phase of the bootstrap process.

Platform Bootstrap Lifecycle Contract

Represents the canonical lifecycle contract for the Platform Bootstrap.

Each lifecycle contains:

Current Lifecycle Status

The lifecycle becomes the authoritative representation of bootstrap progression.

Default Platform Bootstrap Lifecycle

Provides the default lifecycle assigned to newly created Platform Bootstrap instances.

Every newly created Platform Bootstrap begins in:

CREATED

This guarantees deterministic initialization across every deployment.

Platform Bootstrap Lifecycle Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Platform Bootstrap lifecycle definitions.

Responsibilities include:

lifecycle registration
lifecycle discovery
lifecycle enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository for bootstrap lifecycle definitions.

Following the COREI repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Platform Bootstrap Lifecycle Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Lifecycle Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register lifecycle
retrieve lifecycle definitions
enumerate lifecycle instances

Operational lifecycle management remains isolated from repository storage.

Platform Bootstrap Lifecycle Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Lifecycle Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Lifecycle Status
Lifecycle Contract
Default Lifecycle
Lifecycle Registry
Lifecycle Manager

This becomes the official entry point for Platform Bootstrap lifecycle functionality.

or

FAILED

The lifecycle defines progression only.

It does not determine execution.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-14 → STEP-04
Platform Bootstrap Composition

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-04 establishes the Platform Bootstrap Composition, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the structural organization of the Platform Bootstrap.

While STEP-03 established the operational condition of the bootstrap subsystem, STEP-04 introduces the structural composition that defines what components collectively constitute the Platform Bootstrap.

This preserves another COREI Bootstrap architectural principle:

State defines operational condition.
Composition defines structural organization.

The Platform Bootstrap Composition provides standardized composition contracts while remaining independent of dependency management, execution, validation, pipeline processing, orchestration, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Platform Bootstrap
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Execution
        ├── Validation
        ├── Pipeline
        └── Orchestrator

The Composition becomes the fourth institutional layer of the Platform Bootstrap Platform.

3. Purpose

The Platform Bootstrap Composition answers the institutional question:

"What components constitute the Platform Bootstrap?"

Rather than embedding bootstrap components directly inside execution logic, COREI centralizes structural ownership into a dedicated institutional composition platform.

STEP-04 introduces:

Platform Bootstrap Component
Platform Bootstrap Composition
Default Platform Bootstrap Composition
Platform Bootstrap Composition Registry
Platform Bootstrap Composition Manager
Platform Bootstrap Composition Public API

It is not responsible for:

dependency resolution
execution sequencing
validation logic
orchestration
business implementation

Those responsibilities remain delegated to later bootstrap layers.

4. Platform Bootstrap Composition Components

The Composition Platform introduces six institutional components.

Platform Bootstrap Component

Represents a single structural component of the Platform Bootstrap.

Each component defines:

Component Identifier
Component Name

Examples include:

Configuration Loader
Environment Loader
Dependency Loader
Validation Engine
Bootstrap Executor
Readiness Evaluator
Bootstrap Controller

Each component represents a structural building block rather than executable behavior.

Platform Bootstrap Composition

Represents the canonical structural composition of the Platform Bootstrap.

Each composition contains:

Bootstrap Components

The composition becomes the authoritative structural representation of the Platform Bootstrap.

Default Platform Bootstrap Composition

Provides the default composition assigned to newly created Platform Bootstrap instances.

Initially every bootstrap composition contains:

Components

(empty)

Components are registered progressively while preserving the standardized composition contract.

Platform Bootstrap Composition Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for Platform Bootstrap compositions.

Responsibilities include:

composition registration
composition discovery
composition enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository for Platform Bootstrap compositions.

Following the COREI repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Platform Bootstrap Composition Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Composition Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register compositions
retrieve compositions
enumerate composition collections

Operational composition management remains isolated from repository storage.

Platform Bootstrap Composition Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Composition Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Bootstrap Component
Bootstrap Composition
Default Bootstrap Composition
Composition Registry
Composition Manager

This becomes the official entry point for Platform Bootstrap composition functionality.

Platform Bootstrap

        │

Bootstrap Composition

        │

├── Configuration Loader

├── Environment Loader

├── Dependency Loader

├── Validation Engine

├── Bootstrap Executor

├── Readiness Evaluator

└── Bootstrap Controller

The Composition Platform defines structure only.

It does not define execution behavior.

The Composition defines structural organization.

Relationship:

Platform Bootstrap State
            │

Platform Bootstrap Composition

4. Platform Bootstrap Execution Components

The Execution Platform introduces seven institutional components.

Platform Bootstrap Execution Context

Represents the execution environment supplied to the bootstrap runtime.

Each execution context defines:

Execution Identifier
Execution Name

Future platform versions may extend the execution context with:

configuration
environment
runtime metadata
dependency graph
execution state
execution telemetry

The execution context becomes the canonical runtime input.

Platform Bootstrap Runtime

Represents the executable runtime contract.

Every Platform Bootstrap Runtime exposes:

execute()

The runtime defines executable behavior but not execution coordination.

Platform Bootstrap Executor

Provides the institutional implementation of the Platform Bootstrap Runtime.

Responsibilities include:

accepting execution context
executing bootstrap runtime
providing deterministic execution interface

The Executor performs execution without assuming orchestration responsibilities.

Default Platform Bootstrap Execution

Provides the default runtime implementation.

Every newly created Platform Bootstrap receives a default executor implementation.

Future runtime implementations may replace the default executor while preserving the execution contract.

Platform Bootstrap Execution Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for execution runtimes.

Responsibilities include:

execution registration
execution discovery
execution enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository for execution implementations.

Following the COREI repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Platform Bootstrap Execution Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Execution Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register runtime
retrieve runtime
enumerate execution implementations

Operational execution management remains isolated from repository storage.

Platform Bootstrap Execution Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Execution Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Execution Context
Runtime
Executor
Default Execution
Execution Registry
Execution Manager

This becomes the official entry point for Platform Bootstrap execution functionality.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-14 → STEP-07
Platform Bootstrap Validation

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-07 establishes the Platform Bootstrap Validation, the institutional subsystem responsible for verifying that the Platform Bootstrap has executed correctly and that the operating environment satisfies all institutional startup requirements before continuing.

While STEP-06 introduced deterministic bootstrap execution, STEP-07 introduces the validation framework that evaluates the correctness, completeness, and readiness of that execution.

This preserves another COREI Bootstrap architectural principle:

Execution performs work.
Validation verifies work.

The Platform Bootstrap Validation provides standardized validation contracts while remaining independent of pipeline coordination, orchestration, and business implementation.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Platform Bootstrap
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Dependency
        ├── Execution
        ├── Validation
        ├── Pipeline
        └── Orchestrator

The Validation Platform becomes the seventh institutional layer of the Platform Bootstrap Platform.

3. Purpose

The Platform Bootstrap Validation answers the institutional question:

"Did the Platform Bootstrap execute successfully and satisfy institutional startup requirements?"

Rather than allowing every bootstrap component to independently determine whether startup succeeded, COREI centralizes validation into a dedicated institutional platform.

STEP-07 introduces:

Platform Bootstrap Validation Result
Platform Bootstrap Validation Policy
Platform Bootstrap Validator
Default Platform Bootstrap Validation
Platform Bootstrap Validation Registry
Platform Bootstrap Validation Manager
Platform Bootstrap Validation Public API

It is not responsible for:

execution
pipeline coordination
orchestration
business implementation

Those responsibilities remain delegated to the remaining bootstrap layers.

4. Platform Bootstrap Validation Components

The Validation Platform introduces seven institutional components.

Platform Bootstrap Validation Result

Represents the authoritative outcome of bootstrap validation.

The validation model defines four institutional results:

UNKNOWN

PASS

WARNING

FAIL

Each result represents a deterministic evaluation outcome.

Platform Bootstrap Validation Policy

Represents the validation policy applied during bootstrap verification.

Each policy defines:

Validation Identifier
Validation Name

Future implementations may extend policies with:

mandatory validation rules
optional validation rules
severity levels
validation groups
platform-specific validation sets

The policy becomes the canonical validation configuration.

Platform Bootstrap Validator

Represents the institutional validation contract.

Every validator exposes:

validate(context)

The validator evaluates the execution context and returns a deterministic validation result.

Default Platform Bootstrap Validation

Provides the default validation implementation.

The default validator satisfies the validation contract and returns a successful validation result while more advanced validation rules are introduced in later platform stages.

Platform Bootstrap Validation Registry

Provides the centralized institutional repository for validation implementations.

Responsibilities include:

validator registration
validator discovery
validator enumeration

The Registry becomes the authoritative repository for validation implementations.

Following the COREI repository pattern, the Registry exposes a readonly view while maintaining a private mutable collection internally.

Platform Bootstrap Validation Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Validation Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register validators
retrieve validators
enumerate validator implementations

Operational validation management remains isolated from repository storage.

Platform Bootstrap Validation Public API

Provides the institutional export surface for the Validation Platform.

The Public API exposes:

Validation Result
Validation Policy
Validator
Default Validation
Validation Registry
Validation Manager

This becomes the official entry point for Platform Bootstrap validation functionality.

5. Validation Model

The institutional validation model is represented as:

Platform Bootstrap

        │

Execution Context

        │

Validation Policy

        │

Validator

        │

Validation Result

        │

UNKNOWN

PASS

WARNING

FAIL

The Validation Platform evaluates execution.

It does not coordinate execution.

6. Relationship with the Execution Platform

The Execution Platform performs runtime behavior.

The Validation Platform evaluates execution results.

Relationship:

Platform Bootstrap Execution
            │
            ▼
Platform Bootstrap Validation

Execution answers:

How is bootstrap executed?

Validation answers:

Was bootstrap executed correctly?

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

7. Relationship with the Pipeline Platform

The Pipeline Platform consumes validation results.

Relationship:

Platform Bootstrap Validation
            │
            ▼
Platform Bootstrap Pipeline

Validation determines whether execution satisfies institutional requirements.

Pipeline coordinates validated bootstrap stages.

STAGE-25
PHASE-15
Platform Configuration

Institutional Objective

Establish the institutional Platform Configuration subsystem responsible for deterministic configuration definition, loading, validation, composition, dependency management, execution, and orchestration across the COREI Operating System.

Platform Configuration becomes the authoritative configuration layer for every subsystem within COREI, ensuring that all platform services, domains, workflows, terminals, infrastructure components, and runtime engines consume a standardized, version-controlled configuration model.

Unlike environment variables or framework-specific configuration mechanisms, the Platform Configuration subsystem provides an institutional abstraction that separates configuration identity from configuration lifecycle, operational state, structural composition, dependency modeling, execution, validation, pipeline coordination, and orchestration.

This subsystem becomes the single source of truth for platform configuration throughout the COREI Operating System.

Institutional Scope

Platform Configuration consists of nine institutional layers following the standardized COREI subsystem architecture.

Platform Configuration

├── Foundation
├── Lifecycle
├── State
├── Composition
├── Dependency
├── Execution
├── Validation
├── Pipeline
└── Orchestrator

Each layer owns exactly one architectural responsibility.
